I need scarcely say that to come in contact with this shore, even in the finest weather, would involve the certain destruction of any craft in a very few seconds.

===Chinese===
我当年随「猎鹰号」在特林达迪外海的经历使我确信：瀑布外的锚地远比别处安全；说实在的，也只有在这一处才待得住任何时日。须知，船锚泊在特林达迪这样的洋中小岛之外，从来没有真正安全的时候。一旦起风，就要随时准备弃锚，即刻逃向外海。因此必须离岸有一段距离，好有足够的余地朝任何一舷抢风驶出。若是离岸太近，便要冒大风险——这一点我后来沿海岸航行时屡有体会；因为哪怕外面风刮得再猛，崖下却风平浪静，或者一阵阵乱转的风气和旋风轮番作弄，弄得船无法操控，被海涌的推力径直送上致命的礁石。不必说，同这道海岸一沾边，哪怕天气再好，任何船只都注定在几秒钟内毁灭。

===Original===
The anchorage off the cascade possesses many advantages. The coast here is free from any outlying dangers, and there is a depth of five fathoms close to the beach. One cannot be embayed there, for the coast beyond West Point trends away northward almost at right angles to the southwest shore, so that from the anchorage it is easy to get away on either tack, according to the direction of the wind. Here, too, the sea is smoother than anywhere else, except on rare occasions, for the prevailing winds are northeast to southeast, more generally southeast.

===Chinese===
瀑布外的锚地有许多好处。这一带海岸外围没有任何暗险，紧挨着海滩便有五英寻深的水。在这里也不会被困在湾中脱身不得，因为西角以外的海岸几乎成直角向北拐去，与西南岸分岔，所以从锚地出发，视风向如何，朝哪一舷抢风驶出都容易。而且除个别日子外，这里的海面也比别处平静，因为这一带的盛行风是东北风到东南风，更多是东南风。

===Original===
Now, the only other possible anchorage for us would have been in Southwest Bay, in very convenient proximity to our camp; but this, though it might do for a day or two, was absolutely unfitted for a lengthy stay, more especially as difficulties might occur with the vessel while I was on shore myself and only inexperienced people were in charge of her. In this bay one is surrounded by dangers. South Point is on one side, with the current generally setting directly on to it and across the perilous shoals that extend a mile and a half seaward. On the other side is the cape dividing West and Southwest Bays, off which also lie several dangerous islets and rocks. According to the Admiralty chart Southwest Bay itself is quite clean, with a uniform depth of ten fathoms. As a matter of fact, it is full of sunken rocks, and there is an island right in the middle of it; its existence is ignored by all the charts. Surrounded as the bay is by lofty mountains, the winds are very uncertain within it, so that if one should have to weigh anchor it might be difficult to extricate the vessel from her dangerous position even by the exercise of the smartest seamanship. Lastly, it affords no shelter from the prevailing wind, southeast, which often raises a nasty sea, and, what is more, it is entirely exposed to the storm-wind of these seas, the dreaded *pampero*, which blows right into it. Anyone in charge of a vessel brought up in this trap would be compelled to get underway frequently under most difficult circumstances, and would live an unenviable life of perpetual anxiety. This information will, I trust, be of use to any fresh adventurers who propose to hunt for the treasure of Trinidad.

===Chinese===
至于我们唯一可能的另一处锚地，便是西南湾内，紧挨着我们的营地，极是方便；但这地方纵然凑合一两天，绝不宜久留，尤其考虑到：一旦我自己上了岸，船上只有没有经验的人照管，难保不出岔子。在这湾里，人被危险团团围住。一侧是南角，海流照例直冲着它，还扫过向外海延伸一英里半的险恶浅滩。另一侧是分隔西湾与西南湾的海角，角外也卧着几座危险的小岛与礁石。按海军部的海图，西南湾本身干干净净，水深一律十英寻。事实却是，湾里满是暗礁，正当中还有一座岛；所有海图对它的存在都置之不理。这湾既被高山团团围住，湾内的风向便极不可靠，倘若被迫起锚，纵有最高明的船艺，也未必能把船从险境中解脱出来。最后还有一层：湾口对盛行风东南风毫无遮拦，这种风常掀起险恶的海面；更有甚者，它还完全敞向这一带海域的风暴之风——令人闻之色变的帕姆佩罗风，那风正对着湾口灌进来。谁若把船带进这个陷阱里停泊，谁就得在极其困难的情形下频频起锚开船，过一份无人艳羡、永无宁日的心焦日子。这些情况，我相信，对日后有意寻访特林达迪宝藏的新的冒险家们，当会有所用处。

===Original===
Though I would not venture into Southwest Bay with the yacht, I knew that we should have to carry our stores and tools there by boat and land them on the beach opposite to the treasure ravine; for to transport them by land from the easy landing-place near the cascade would be an almost impossible undertaking.

===Chinese===
尽管我不愿驾游艇冒险进西南湾，我却知道，我们势必得用小艇把给养和工具运过去，在正对宝藏裂谷的滩头卸下；因为若从瀑布近旁那处便利的登陆点由陆路搬运过去，几乎是不可能办到的事。

===Original===
According to the dead pirate's statement, he and his comrades had surveyed Southwest Bay and discovered the best channel between the rocks. He gave the directions for finding this channel to Captain P——, and its existence had been verified by both the South Shields explorers; but as they had brought back an alarming account of its dangers, and boats had been lost in it, I considered that it would be a wise precaution for me to land at the pier in the first place, walk—or rather crawl and climb, for there is not much walking to be done on that journey—across the island and survey Southwest Bay from the hills above it, before attempting to beach a boat there.

===Chinese===
按照那死去海盗的说法，他和同伙曾勘察过西南湾，找到了礁石之间最好的水道。他把寻找这条水路的方位告诉了 P——船长，南希尔兹来的两位探险者也证实过它确实存在；但他们带回来的危险报告令人心惊，又有小艇曾折损在那里面，因此我认定，稳妥起见，还是先在天然码头登岸，徒步——不如说爬行和攀援吧，因为那趟路程实在没有多少可「走」的余地——横穿此岛，从湾上方的山头察看西南湾，然后再设法让小艇抢滩。

===Original===
In the evening we held a council in the saloon over our pipes, and I explained my plans for the following day.

===Chinese===
晚上，我们在舱厅里叼着烟斗开了个议事会，我把次日的计划讲了一遍。

===Original===
I had explored the island pretty thoroughly while here before, and I knew that it mainly consisted of inaccessible peaks and precipices, among which there were very few passes practicable for men. In many places the cliffs fall precipitously into the sea, affording no foothold. I had landed in both Northwest Bay and the bay beyond it, and, though there were sandy beaches in both these, still, one could go no further, for sheer promontories on either side and mountains equally insurmountable at the back cut off all communication between these coves and the rest of the island. I also knew that it would be impossible for me to walk along the beach from the pier to Southwest Bay, for between these were the two capes that bound West Bay, both opposing barriers of precipices to one's advance.

===Chinese===
上次在此时我已把这岛探得相当透彻，知道它大半由不可攀越的峰峦与峭壁构成，其间可供人通行的隘口寥寥无几。许多地方峭壁直插入海，连立足之地也没有。西北湾和再往外的那处海湾我都曾登过岸；两湾虽都有沙滩，人却到此为止再进不得，因为两侧是刀削般的海岬，背后是同样翻不过去的大山，把这些小湾同全岛其余地方之间的通路割了个干净。我也知道，想从天然码头沿着海滩走到西南湾是决计不能的，因为这两处之间夹着西湾两侧的那两座海角，它们都以峭壁之障横挡着来路。

===Original===
But while here with the *Falcon*, after a difficult and dangerous search which has been fully described in the narrative of that voyage, I at last discovered a pass, and I believe it is the only one, by which the mountains at the centre of the island can be traversed and the windward shore attained.

===Chinese===
不过，当年随「猎鹰号」在此的时候，经过一番曲折危险的搜寻——那番经历在那次航行的记叙里已写得详详细细——我终于发现了一道隘口，而且我相信这是唯一的一道，由此可以翻过岛中央的群山，抵达向风一岸。

===Original===
First, I ascended the steep ravine down which the cascade flows. Having arrived at the summit of the ravine I crossed the groves of tree-ferns, and, after making several descents into ravines which terminated in precipices and so compelled me to retrace my steps, I succeeded in discovering a gully which led me to the beach on the northeast side of the island. From here I found it possible to walk along the beach to South Point, for no insurmountable capes intervened; and from Southeast Bay there was an easy pass under the Sugarloaf Mountain by which the Treasure Bay could be reached. This was the journey which I intended to make once again on the following morning. This route, together with others taken in the course of our explorations, are I believe the only accessible ways on the island.

===Chinese===
先沿着瀑布流下的那道陡峭裂谷攀上去。到了谷顶，穿过去那片树蕨林；几次下到别处的谷里，谷底却是绝壁，只好原路折返；如此几番之后，我终于寻到一条沟壑，把我引到了岛东北侧的滩头。从那里我发现可以沿海滩一路走到南角，因为中间没有翻不过去的海角；而从东南湾，在糖面包山（Sugarloaf Mountain）脚下有一道好走的隘口，循着它可以到达宝藏湾。这便是我打算次日清晨再走一遍的路。这条路线，加上我们踏勘过程中走过的其他几条，我相信已是全岛仅有的可通行之路。

===Original===
I knew by experience that the passage over the mountains to the windward beach was both arduous and perilous, and that to climb to Southwest Bay, survey it, and return to the pier would occupy the best part of three days.

===Chinese===
我有切身经验，知道翻山到向风滩头的这条路既艰辛又凶险；要爬到西南湾，察看一番，再回到天然码头，非得花上将近三天的工夫。

===Original===
The doctor volunteered to accompany me, and I decided to take him with me. It was indeed important that he should make himself acquainted with the pass, for it had been settled that whenever I remained with the yacht he should be in command of the party working on shore, and, as the only reliable water-supply I knew of was at the cascade, it might become necessary for him to lead the men across the mountains to it should a water-famine occur at Southwest Bay. Again, it was certain that bad weather would occasionally make the landing of boats at Southwest Bay impossible for weeks at a time, so that, if there were some urgent reason for communicating with the yacht, this could only be done by crossing to the pier landing-place, at which I am of opinion that one can land ten times with safety to once in Southwest Bay. It had been my intention to form a depot of stores at the pier, but this we found to be unnecessary.

===Chinese===
医生自告奋勇要同我一起去，我便决定带上他。让他熟悉这条隘口确实要紧，因为已经议定：凡我留在艇上的日子，岸上作业的一队人便归他指挥；而据我所知，唯一可靠的水源在瀑布那里，万一西南湾闹起水荒，就得由他领着人翻山取水。再者，坏天气难免有时一连几周叫小艇无法在西南湾登岸；倘若有紧急事由必须与游艇联络，那也只有翻山到天然码头的登陆点去——依我看，在那里登岸十次有十次平安，而在西南湾，十次里能有一次就算好了。我原打算在码头那边设一处给养囤栈，后来发现并无必要。

===Original===
After I had made the above explanations to my companions assembled in the saloon, our sportsman, who had been listening attentively, remarked: "Skipper, you have given us plenty of reason for taking Cloete-Smith with you tomorrow and teaching him the roads; but you have omitted the most important reason of all. Let me inform you that you won't get us to do any work on shore on Sundays; so on every Sunday afternoon we will put on our best clothes and the doctor will have to take us over the pass to the pier, where we can do a sort of church-parade, and listen to the band. I suppose there will be a bar there, too, with Theodosius as barman presiding over the rum-barrel."

===Chinese===
我向聚在舱厅里的同伴们作完上述解释之后，我们的渔猎好手一直在洗耳恭听，这时开言道：「船长，您讲了一大堆理由，说明明天为什么该带上克卢特-史密斯、教他认路；可您漏掉了最要紧的一条。让我告诉您吧：礼拜天您可别想叫我们在岸上干活；所以每个礼拜天下午，我们都要穿上最好的衣裳，让医生领我们过隘口去码头，在那里来一场教堂游行，再听听乐队演奏。我想那儿多半还该有个酒馆，由西奥多西乌斯（Theodosius）当掌柜，看管那桶朗姆酒。」

## X / 十

===Original===
The Summit of Trinidad

===Chinese===
特林达迪之巅

===Original===
On the following morning﻿—November 21﻿—as soon as breakfast was over, the doctor and myself started for the shore. In view of the rough climbing before us we did not burden ourselves with much baggage, but set forth in light marching order. We dispensed with blankets, and, in addition to the somewhat scanty clothing we had on, we carried merely provisions for three days, consisting of some ship’s biscuit, a few strips of Brazilian *ch’arki* or jerked beef﻿—rather rank﻿—some dried figs, a flask of rum, a tin bottle to hold water, one pannikin, tobacco, pipes, and matches.

===Chinese===
次日清晨——11 月 21 日——早饭一吃完，我和医生便动身往岸上去。想到前路攀爬艰苦，我们没有让多少行装拖累自己，只以轻装进发。毯子一概免了；除了身上那点颇为单薄的衣裳，我们只带了三天的口粮：几块船用硬面饼，几条巴西产的「恰尔基」（ch'arki）风干牛肉——味道相当冲——一些无花果干，一瓶朗姆酒，一只装水用的白铁瓶，一只小铁杯，外加烟草、烟斗和火柴。

===Original===
We could see from the deck that there was considerable surf on the beach, and it was evident that we should not find the landing at the pier to be so easy a matter as it often is.

===Chinese===
从甲板上便望得见滩头浪涛不小；显然，这一次在天然码头登岸，不会像往常那样容易。

===Original===
Two of the paid hands pulled us off in the dinghy. When we were about halfway to the shore we perceived a bright red object on an eminence near the cascade. On getting nearer we distinguished this to be a ragged red flag flying from a pole. This was a startling discovery for us, and might signify that some rival expedition had landed on the island.

===Chinese===
两名雇来的水手划着小艇送我们过去。行到半途，我们望见瀑布近旁的一处高地上有个鲜红的物件。再驶近些才辨清，那是一面从旗杆上飘出的破烂红旗。这一发现叫我们心头一凛：说不定有哪支和我们竞逐宝藏的探险队，已经登上了此岛。

===Original===
We reached the pier and found a high swell rolling by it, while eddies and overfalls round the outer end of it caused the boat to become more or less unmanageable, driving her first in one direction, then in another, so that she could not be brought very close to, without risk of staving her in against the rocks.

===Chinese===
我们到了码头跟前，只见一道高涌从旁滚过；码头外端四周的涡流与壅水搅得小艇多少不听使唤，一会儿把它推向这边，一会儿又推向那边，没法让它靠得太近——否则难保不撞上礁石，把艇壳撞得粉碎。

===Original===
Under these circumstances the only safe method of getting on shore was to jump into the water. The boat was backed in towards the pier end, the men pulling a few strokes ahead whenever a wave threatened to dash her on to it. I stood in the stern and awaited a favourable opportunity, then jumped overboard and clambered quickly up the pier side before the next roller should wash me off. Then the boat was backed in again, and the doctor repeated the performance.

===Chinese===
在这样的情形下，唯一稳妥的登岸办法就是跳进水里。小艇倒退着向码头一端凑近，每当一道浪眼看要把它掼到码头上，水手们便向前划几桨避开。我站在艇尾，候一个合适的机会，随即纵身跳下水去，赶在下一道浪把我冲走之前，迅速攀上码头侧沿。然后小艇又倒退进来，医生如法炮制了一遍。

===Original===
We had no particular objection to the wetting we had received, but a good many of our biscuits were converted into a pulp and our figs were pickled with the seawater.

===Chinese===
挨了这场水淋，我们倒不很在意，只是许多面饼泡成了一摊糊浆，无花果干也让海水腌了个透。

===Original===
So here we were at last safely on shore at Trinidad, both in high spirits at the prospect before us, for we were eager to commence the exploration that might result in who could tell what magnificent results.

===Chinese===
就这样，我们终于平安登上了特林达迪的岸，两人都为眼前的展望而兴致勃发——我们都巴不得立刻开始这场勘探，谁知道它会带来何等辉煌的结果呢。

===Original===
Climbing over the rugged top of the pier we descended on the beach, which at high-water is partly overflowed, the pier being then converted into an island. We scrambled over the rocks and scoria to the height by the cascade on which the flag was, and then our suspicions were put at rest by what we discovered. A good-sized barrel had been firmly jammed between the rocks in a prominent place and filled with stones. A pole had been planted in the barrel, and from this floated the red flag we had seen. It was in so ragged a condition that it was impossible to say whether it had ever been a British flag or not. Under it was a wooden tablet, on which was painted the following inscription: “*H.M.S. Ruby*, February 26, 1889.” There was also a bottle on the cask containing the cards of the commander of the vessel, Captain Kennedy, and his wardroom officers.

===Chinese===
翻过码头粗砺的顶面，我们下到滩上；涨潮时滩头有一部分没入水中，码头那时便成了一座小岛。我们手脚并用地爬过岩石与火山渣，登上瀑布旁那面插着旗子的高地，眼前所见顿时打消了我们的疑虑。原来是一只挺大的木桶，牢牢卡在醒目处的岩缝之间，桶里填满石块，桶中竖起一根旗杆，我们看到的那面红旗便从杆上飘出。旗子破烂得厉害，已无从辨认它究竟是否曾是一面英国旗。旗下挂着一块木牌，牌上写着这样的题记：「皇家海军『红宝石号』（H.M.S. Ruby），1889 年 2 月 26 日。」桶上还系着一只瓶子，里面装着该舰指挥官肯尼迪舰长（Captain Kennedy）与他麾下军官们的名片。

===Original===
Having thus satisfied ourselves that no enemy was in possession of the island, we went to the cascade. This stream rises among the tree-ferns at the summit of the mountain and rushes down the gully with a considerable volume of water. This issue is, I should imagine, perennial.

===Chinese===
既已确知并无敌人占据此岛，我们便走向瀑布。这股水在山顶的树蕨之间发源，携着相当大的水量沿着沟壑奔泻而下。我想，这股水源是终年不涸的。

===Original===
Then we commenced our ascent, which involved no light work. The gully was excessively steep. We were climbing up a staircase of great rocks, and often where there were insurmountable precipices we had to make a detour round the mountainside, creeping carefully along the steep declivities that overhung the cliffs, the rock and earth crumbling beneath our feet as we went: for one of the most unpleasant peculiarities of this island is that it is nowhere solid; it is rotten throughout, its substance has been disintegrated by volcanic fires and by the action of water, so that it is everywhere tumbling to pieces. As one travels over the mountains one is ever starting miniature landslips and dislodging great stones, which roll, thundering, down the cliffs, gathering other companions as they go until a very avalanche is formed. On this day the doctor, who was a little ahead of me at the time, sent adrift a stone weighing a hundredweight at the least, which just cleared my head as I stooped down to dodge it. We were on a dangerous part of the mountain, and had it struck me it must have impelled me over a precipice several hundred feet in height. After this we followed parallel tracks wherever this was feasible.

===Chinese===
接着我们开始攀登，这可不是一桩轻省活计。沟壑陡得出奇。我们等于在攀一道巨岩砌成的阶梯，而每逢前方现出翻不过去的绝壁，就得绕到山坡侧旁迂回，贴着悬临崖顶的陡坡小心翼翼地爬行，一路走，脚下的岩石与泥土簌簌崩落——因为这岛最叫人不痛快的一个怪处，就在于它没有一个地方结实：它从里到外都朽了，山体早被地底的火山之火与流水的作用蚀得支离破碎，处处都在坍塌剥落。人在山间行走，时时踩出小小的滑坡，撞落巨大的石块；石块轰隆隆滚下悬崖，一路裹挟上更多的同伴，最后竟汇成一场货真价实的雪崩。这一天，走在我前头不远的医生蹬松了一块少说有一英担重的石头，我慌忙俯身躲避，它堪堪擦着我的头顶飞过。我们正处在这座山的凶险地段，那石头若砸中我，势必把我掼下几百英尺高的绝壁。打这以后，只要可行，我们都各走各的平行路线，绝不再前后相随。

===Original===
The unstableness of Trinidad causes a perpetual sense of insecurity while one is on the mountains. One knows not when some overhanging pinnacle may topple down. One great source of danger is that there are many declivities which can be descended but not ascended, and it would be easy to get hopelessly imprisoned at the foot of one of these. In the *Cruise of the Falcon* is described one really terrible experience we went through. Our exploring party had found no water, and the boy was practically dying of thirst. So, driven by urgent necessity﻿—for we saw by the configuration of the mountains that we should almost certainly find water at the bottom of a certain ravine﻿—we proceeded to descend to it down a great slope, not of loose debris, but of half-consolidated volcanic matter like half-baked bricks, and very brittle.

===Chinese===
特林达迪这般松动易坍，使人身在山上时永远怀着一份不安全感。谁也说不准哪一块悬空的尖峰会在什么时候倾塌下来。危险的一大来源，是岛上许多陡坡下得去而上不来，人很容易被困在某道坡脚，万难脱身。《猎鹰号巡航记》里写过我们经历过的一桩着实可怕的往事：当时我们那支勘探队遍寻不着水，随行的少年几乎要渴死了。于是为情势所迫——因为我们从山势看出，某道裂谷的谷底几乎肯定有水——我们便顺着一片大坡往下走；那坡不是松散的碎石堆，而是半凝结的火山物质，宛如烧得半熟的砖，脆得很。

===Original===
This slope became steeper as we advanced and very dangerous, but it was impossible to retrace our steps. When we attempted to ascend, the mountain slid away under our feet, crumbling into ashes. It was like climbing a treadmill. So we had to abandon this hope and go still further down, lying on our backs, progressing inch by inch carefully, one of us occasionally sliding down a few yards and sending an avalanche before him. We knew not to the edge of what precipices this dreadful way would lead us. Luckily we reached the bottom and found water in safety. I determined not to get into any difficulties of this description in the course of our present journey.

===Chinese===
我们越往下走，这坡越陡、越凶险，而要原路折返已不可能。我们试图往上攀，山体便从脚底滑走，碎成灰烬，这活像在攀一架踏车。我们只得断了这念想，继续往下，仰面躺着，一寸一寸小心挪动；偶尔有一人滑下几码，身前便卷起一阵雪崩般的石流。我们不知道这条可怕的路终会把我们引到哪一道悬崖的边上。所幸我们终于到达谷底，安然寻到了水。我打定主意，此番旅程，再不让自己陷进这等困境。

===Original===
We gradually ascended the ravine, sometimes climbing on one side of it, sometimes on the other, and occasionally wading through the water at the bottom, according to which route was the safest.

===Chinese===
我们顺着裂谷渐渐上攀，一时攀在谷的这一侧，一时攀在那一侧，偶尔也从谷底的水里蹚过，全看哪条道最稳妥。

===Original===
The nature of the scenery around us was now grand in the extreme, and had a weird character of its own that I have never perceived on other mountains. The jagged and torn peaks, the profound chasms, the huge landslips of black rocks, the slopes of red volcanic ash destitute of vegetation, in themselves produce a sense of extreme desolation; but this is heightened by the presence of a ghastly dead vegetation and by the numberless uncanny birds and land-crabs which cover all the rocks.

===Chinese===
此时四围的景致壮丽已极，自带着一种我在别处山岳上从未感到的诡异气质。狰狞撕裂的峰峦，深不见底的沟壑，黑岩堆成的巨大滑坡，寸草不生的红色火山灰坡——这一切本身已足以生出极度的荒凉之感；而满目死气沉沉的枯萎植被，还有爬满所有岩石、数也数不清的怪鸟与陆蟹，更把这份荒凉又推深一层。

===Original===
This lonely islet is perhaps the principal breeding place for seabirds in the South Atlantic. Here multitudes of man-of-war birds, gannets, boobies, cormorants, and petrels have their undisturbed haunts. Not knowing how dangerous he is, they treat their superior animal, man, with a shocking want of due respect. The large birds more especially attack one furiously if one approaches their nests in the breeding season, and in places where one has to clamber with hands as well as feet, and is therefore helpless, they are positively dangerous.

===Chinese===
这座孤岛或许是南大西洋数一数二的海鸟繁殖地。成群的军舰鸟（man-of-war birds）、塘鹅、鲣鸟、鸬鹚和海燕在此营巢栖止，不受惊扰。它们不晓得人这种动物何等危险，对待自己这位更高等的动物——人——竟然放肆得毫无半分敬意，实在叫人吃惊。尤其到了繁殖季节，人若走近它们的巢，那些大鸟更是凶猛扑击；在须得手脚并用攀爬、因而腾不出手的地方，它们当真可以伤人。

===Original===
As for the land-crabs, which are unlike any I have seen elsewhere, they swarm all over the island in incredible numbers. I have even seen them two or three deep in shady places under the rocks; they crawl over everything, polluting every stream, devouring anything﻿—a loathsome lot of brutes, which were of use, however, round our camp as scavengers. They have hard shells of a bright saffron colour, and their faces have a most cynical and diabolic expression. As one approaches them they stand on their hind legs and wave their pincers threateningly, while they roll their hideous goggle eyes at one in a dreadful manner. If a man is sleeping or sitting down quietly, these creatures will come up to have a bite at him, and would devour him if he was unable for some reason to shake them off; but we murdered so many in the vicinity of our camp during our stay on the island, that they certainly became less bold, and it seemed almost as if the word had been passed all over Trinidad that we were dangerous animals, to be shunned by every prudent crab. Even when we were exploring remote districts we at last found that they fled in terror, instead of menacing us with their claws.

===Chinese===
至于陆蟹，与我别处见过的全然不同，它们漫山遍野爬满全岛，数目多得难以置信。我甚至在岩石底下背阴的地方见过它们叠了两三层；它们爬遍一切，弄脏每一条溪流，见什么吃什么——一伙面目可憎的畜生；不过在营地四周，它们倒也充当了清道夫的角色。它们生着藏红色亮壳，一张脸透着十足的刻毒与邪魔的神气。人一走近，它们便后腿直立，威吓地挥舞双螯，同时把那双丑恶的凸眼朝人骨碌碌乱转，模样骇人。人若睡着或安安静静坐着，这些家伙也会凑上来咬一口，倘若那人因故甩不脱它们，真会被它们啃了去；好在我们居留岛上的那阵子，在营地附近结果了那么多只，它们确实收敛了许多——几乎像全特林达迪都传开了信儿，说我们是危险的动物，凡行事谨慎的螃蟹都当避而远之。到后来，即便我们在偏远地带踏勘，也发现它们只顾惊惶逃命，再不敢举螯示威了。

===Original===
But the great mystery of this mysterious island is the forest of dead trees which covers it and which astonishes every visitor.

===Chinese===
然而，这座神秘之岛最大的谜，还是覆盖全岛的那片枯死树林，它令每一位来客惊诧不已。

===Original===
The following account of this wood is taken from the *Cruise of the Falcon*, and as it was nine years ago, so is it now:﻿—

===Chinese===
下面这段关于这片树林的记述引自《猎鹰号巡航记》；九年前如此，如今依然：——

===Original===
“What struck us as remarkable was, that though in this cove there was no live vegetation of any kind, there were traces of an abundant extinct vegetation. The mountain slopes were thickly covered with dead wood﻿—wood, too, that had evidently long since been dead; some of these leafless trunks were prostrate, some still stood up as they had grown.﻿ ﻿… When we afterwards discovered that over the whole of this extensive island﻿—from the beach up to the summit of the highest mountain﻿—at the bottom and on the slopes of every now barren ravine, on whose loose-rolling stones no vegetation could possibly take root﻿—these dead trees were strewed as closely as it is possible for trees to grow; and when we further perceived that they all seemed to have died at one and the same time, as if plague-struck, and that no single live specimen, young or old, was to be found anywhere﻿—our amazement was increased.

===Chinese===
「有一桩事情令我们称奇：这片小湾里虽没有任何一种活着的植被，却处处有繁盛而今已绝灭的植物的痕迹。山坡上密密覆盖着枯木——而且是显然早已枯死多年的木头；有的无叶树干倒伏于地，有的仍像生长时那样直立着。……后来我们发现，在这座大岛的全境——从海滩直到最高峰的峰顶——在每一道如今光秃的裂谷的谷底和坡上，在那些滚石松动、植物根本无从扎根之处——这些死树密密铺陈，密到了树木所能生长的极限；再看到它们似乎全都死于同一时刻，像遭了瘟疫一般，而且无论老幼，任何地方连一棵活的也找不出来——我们的惊异愈发加深了。

===Original===
“At one time Trinidad must have been covered with one magnificent forest, presenting to passing vessels a far different appearance to that it now does, with its inhospitable and barren crags.

===Chinese===
「特林达迪想必曾有一个时期被一片壮丽的森林所覆盖，那时过往船只所见的景象，与今日这荒凉贫瘠、拒人千里的嶙峋危岩相比，定然截然不同。

===Original===
“The descriptions given in the *Directory* allude to these forests; therefore, whatever catastrophe it may have been that killed off all the vegetation of the island, it must have occurred within the memory of man.

===Chinese===
「《航路指南》（the Directory）中的描述提到过这些森林；因此，无论当年是什么灾变杀死了岛上的全部植被，它必定发生在人类的记忆之内。

===Original===
“Looking at the rotten, broken up condition of the rock, and the nature of the soil, where there is a soil﻿—a loose powder, not consolidated like earth, but having the appearance of fallen volcanic ash﻿—I could not help imagining that some great eruption had brought about all this desolation; Trinidad is the acknowledged centre of a small volcanic patch that lies in this portion of the South Atlantic, therefore I think this theory a more probable one than that of a long drought, a not very likely contingency in this rather rainy region.”

===Chinese===
「看看岩石那朽烂崩碎的状态，再看看土壤——凡是有土壤的地方——的性质：一种松散的粉末，不像泥土那样凝结，倒像是落定的火山灰——我不禁设想，是一场大喷发酿成了这满目荒凉；特林达迪是南大西洋此处一小片火山地带的公认中心，因此我认为这一说法比长期干旱之说更为可信——在这片相当多雨的地区，长期干旱原就不是什么大概率的事。」

===Original===
Some time after the publication of the *Cruise of the Falcon* I came across an excellent description of Trinidad in Captain Marryat’s novel, *Frank Mildmay*. It is obvious from the following passage, which I quote from that work, that the trees had been long dead at the date of its publication, 1829:﻿—

===Chinese===
《猎鹰号巡航记》出版之后过了些时日，我偶然在马里亚特船长（Captain Marryat）的小说《弗兰克·米尔徳梅》（Frank Mildmay）里读到一段对特林达迪的出色描写。从下面引自该书的一段文字可以明白看出：在该书出版的 1829 年，这些树便已枯死多年了：——

===Original===
“Here a wonderful and most melancholy phenomenon arrested our attention. Thousands and thousands of trees covered the valley, each of them about thirty feet high; but every tree was dead, and extended its leafless boughs to another﻿—a forest of desolation, as if nature had at some particular moment ceased to vegetate! There was no underwood or grass. On the lowest of the dead boughs, the gannets, and other seabirds, had built their nests, in numbers uncountable. Their tameness, as Cowper says, ‘was shocking to me.’ So unaccustomed did they seem to man that the mothers brooding over their young only opened their beaks, in a menacing attitude, at us as we passed by them. How to account satisfactorily for the simultaneous destruction of this vast forest of trees was very difficult; there was no want of rich earth for nourishment of the roots. The most probable cause appeared to me a sudden and continued eruption of sulphuric effluvia from the volcano; or else by some unusually heavy gale of wind or hurricane the trees had been drenched with salt water to the roots. One or the other of these causes must have produced the effect. The philosopher or the geologist must decide.”

===Chinese===
「这里有一桩奇妙而极其凄凉的现象攫住了我们的注意。千千万万棵树覆盖着山谷，每棵约三十英尺高；然而每一棵都是死的，光秃秃的枝桠伸向彼此——一片荒凉的森林，仿佛大自然在某个特定的时刻骤然停止了生发！没有下木，也没有青草。在最低的那些枯枝上，塘鹅和别的海鸟筑了巢，数目不可胜数。正如柯珀（Cowper）所说，它们不怕人的样子『真叫我惊骇』。它们似乎太不习惯于人，以致伏在幼鸟身上的母鸟只朝我们张开嘴，摆出一副恫吓的姿态，任我们从旁走过。要圆满解释这片浩大森林为何同时毁灭，颇为困难；滋养树根的沃土并不匮乏。依我看，最可能的缘故，一是火山突然而持续的硫磺喷气；不然便是一场异常猛烈的大风或飓风，把咸澀的海水一直浇到树根。二者必居其一，其因不出于此。这该留待哲学家或地质学家来裁断。」

===Original===
Captain Marryat was evidently unaware that these dead trees are to be found on the heights 3,000 feet above the sea-level as well as in the valleys, or he would not have suggested salt water as the cause of their destruction.

===Chinese===
马里亚特船长显然不知道，这些死树不但在谷中就有，在海拔 3,000 英尺的高处也能见到，否则他就不会把海水说成它们毁灭的原因了。

===Original===
His description proves that the trees were dead at least sixty years ago, and in all probability they had been dead for a long time before. The latest record I have been able to discover which describes live trees as existing on Trinidad is dated as far back as 1700. The Ninepin and the Sugarloaf, now utterly barren, were then crowded with trees of a great size.

===Chinese===
他的描述证明，这些树至少在六十年前便已死去，而且多半在那之前许久就已枯亡。我能找到的、记载特林达迪尚有活树的最新一份记录，其年份远至 1700 年。今日彻底荒秃的「九柱石」和糖面包山，当年都长满了高大的树木。

===Original===
Though some of this timber is rotten, a large proportion of it is not decayed in the least, but when cut with the axe presents the appearance of a sound, well-seasoned wood. It is gnarled and knotty, extremely hard and heavy, its specific gravity being but slightly less than that of water. It is of a dark reddish colour and of very close grain.

===Chinese===
这些木材虽有朽坏的部分，很大一部分却丝毫没有腐坏，斧头砍开，断面宛如完好风干的良材。它盘曲多节，极其坚硬沉重，比重只比水略小。颜色暗红，纹理十分致密。

===Original===
I brought a log of it home and sent it to a cabinetmaker, who found that it would take an excellent polish. On sending this specimen to Kew I was informed that the wood “probably belongs to the family Myrtaceae, and possibly to the species Eugenia.” I find that this species includes the pimento or allspice, the rose-apple, and other aromatic and fruit-producing trees; so that desert Trinidad may at one time have been a delicious spice-island.

===Chinese===
我带了一根原木回家，送给一位细木匠，他发现这木头能打磨出极好的光泽。把这件标本寄到丘园（Kew）之后，我被告知，这种木材「可能属于桃金娘科（Myrtaceae），或系番樱桃属（Eugenia）的某个种」。我查知这一属包括多香果树（pimento，又称 allspice）、蒲桃（rose-apple），以及其他芬芳和结果的树木；如此说来，今日荒瘠如沙漠的特林达迪，或许曾是一座馥郁可人的香料之岛。

===Original===
The doctor and myself toiled on up the gully, whose slopes, as we approached the summit, became less rugged, and here the ferns grew up between the trunks of the dead trees, spreading wide their beautiful fronds of fresh green.

===Chinese===
我和医生在沟壑里继续奋力上攀；将近山顶时，两侧坡面渐见平缓，蕨类在枯树的躯干之间生长出来，舒展开鲜绿而美丽的叶羽。

===Original===
When we had come to a spot a little below the source of the stream we left the gully﻿—not before we had drunk our fill and replenished the bottle﻿—and ascended the down where the tree-ferns grow thickest. The soil is here very loose and presents the appearance of having been quite recently ploughed up, while it is honeycombed with the holes of the teeming land-crabs.

===Chinese===
走到溪源略下一点的地方，我们便离开沟壑——临行前先喝了个痛快，把水瓶也灌满——登上那片树蕨长得最密的草冈。这里的土质极松，看上去就像新近被犁过似的，而且千疮百孔，布满了滋生不息的陆蟹打下的洞。

===Original===
Soon we reached the summit of the plateau, where a pleasant breeze stirred the ferns and we could now command a magnificent view not only over the mountains we had climbed but over the weather side of the island as well. I remembered the scene, for I had looked down from here nine years before. On the weather side of the island the mountains are even more precipitous than on the lee side; but, on the other hand, they do not run sheer into the sea, for at their base extend great green slopes continued by broad sandy beaches. Along all this coast are shallow flats and outlying rocks on which the surf breaks perpetually. Thirty miles out to sea rise the inaccessible rocky islets of Martin Vas.

===Chinese===
不久我们登上了这片高台的顶巅。怡人的微风吹动蕨叶，从这里不仅可以俯瞰我们一路攀过的群山，连岛的向风一面也尽收眼底。我记得这景象，因为九年前我便从这里眺望过。岛的向风面，山势比背风面更加陡峭；不过话说回来，那边的山并不笔直插入海中，山脚下延展着大片绿色草坡，下接宽阔的沙滩。整条海岸沿线尽是浅滩与外露的礁石，浪涛在上面终年不歇地拍碎。向外海三十英里，耸起人迹难至的岩岛——马丁瓦斯群岛（Martin Vas）。

===Original===
The plateau we were on was covered with a luxuriant vegetation, for in addition to the tree-ferns there were large bushes of some species of acacia﻿—a tall thorny plant with flowers like those of scarlet-runners, and bearing large beans﻿—flowering grasses, and various other plants. I collected specimens of these later on, which were lost, however, with other stores shortly before we abandoned the island, in consequence of the capsizing of our boat while launching her in Treasure Bay.

===Chinese===
我们所在的高台覆盖着繁茂的植被：除了树蕨，还有成大丛的某种金合欢——一种高大多刺的植物，开的花像红花菜豆的花，结着大的豆荚——又有抽穗开花的草类，以及其他种种植物。这些我后来都采集了标本，可惜在弃岛前不久，小艇在宝藏湾下水时倾覆，标本同其他给养一并损失了。

===Original===
It seemed strange to find so beautiful a garden, high up, almost unapproachable for the perils that surround it, throned as it is on a wilderness of rock rising up to it in chaotic masses and sheer precipices from the shore far below. The sailors under Frank Mildmay discovered this grove before me. In all his descriptions of places and scenery Captain Marryat is singularly faithful to the truth, even in the minutest details. In this respect indeed he is more conscientious in his works of fiction than are most travellers in their presumedly true narratives. The most minute and accurate description of Trinidad that I have come across is in *Frank Mildmay*, and it is easy to identify every spot mentioned in that book. The author must himself have visited this strange place, and his imagination was strongly stirred by it. He gives us graphic pictures of “the iron-bound coast with high and pointed rocks, frowning defiance over the unappeasable and furious waves which break incessantly at their feet.” His hero also experiences the usual difficulty in landing; men and boat are nearly lost, and in all his thrilling narrative there is not the least exaggeration. All the events described might well have happened, and probably did happen.

===Chinese===
在这样高的地方竟有如此美丽的一座花园，四围险象环生，几乎无从企及——它端坐在一片荒岩的宝座之上，那片荒岩从底下远远的海滩拔起，乱石成堆、绝壁直上，一路拥到它的脚下。弗兰克·米尔徳梅手下的水手们比我更早发现了这片树丛。马里亚特船长描写地方与景色时极其忠于真实，连最细枝末节之处也不含糊。在这一点上，他写虚构小说确实比大多数旅行家写号称纪实的游记更加诚实。我读到过的对特林达迪最细致、最准确的描述，就数《弗兰克·米尔徳梅》里那段，书中提到的每一处地点都容易对号入座。作者必定亲自到过这个奇异之地，他的想象力也为此深深激荡。他为我们绘声绘色地描画出「铁岸森然，高岩尖耸，对着脚下永不平息、狂怒不止、频频拍击的巨浪怒目相抗」。他笔下的主人公登岸时也尝了寻常的那份艰难，人与小艇几乎俱遭不测；而他通篇惊心动魄的叙述里，没有丝毫夸张。所写的一切事件都完全可能发生过，多半也确实发生过。

===Original===
Of the grove he says:﻿—“The men reported that they had gained the summit of the mountain, where they had discovered a large plain, skirted by a species of fern-tree from twelve to eighteen feet high﻿—that on this plain they had seen a herd of goats; and among them could distinguish one of enormous size which appeared to be their leader. They also found many wild hogs.”

===Chinese===
关于这片树丛，他写道：——「水手们报告说，他们登上了山顶，在那里发现一大片平原，四缘镶着一道十二到十八英尺高的树蕨——平原上他们望见一群山羊，其中能认出一头硕大无朋的，似乎是它们的领队。他们还发现许多野猪。」

===Original===
We saw no goats or hogs, and I am confident that none are now left alive. We did, however, in the course of our digging discover what appeared to be the bones of a goat. It is well known that these animals once abounded here. Captain Halley, of the *Paramore Pink*, afterwards Dr. Halley, Astronomer-Royal, landed on this island April 17, 1700, and put on it some goats and hogs for breeding, as also a pair of guinea-fowl which he carried from St. Helena. “I took,” says his journal, “possession of the island in his Majesty’s name, as knowing it to be granted by the King’s letters-patent, leaving the Union Jack flying.”

===Chinese===
我们一只山羊或野猪也没见到，我确信如今一只活口也不剩了。不过在挖掘过程中，我们确实发现了看似山羊的骸骨。众所周知，这些动物曾在此地繁衍极多。1700 年 4 月 17 日，「帕拉莫尔·平克号」（Paramore Pink）的哈雷船长（Captain Halley）——即日后出任皇家天文学家的哈雷博士——在此岛登岸，放上了一批供繁育的山羊和野猪，还放了一对从圣赫勒拿（St. Helena）带来的珠鸡。「我以国王陛下之名，」他的日志写道，「占领了此岛，因知它已蒙国王特许状授予，离去时留下了飘扬的联合王国旗。」

===Original===
The American commander, Amaso Delano, visited Trinidad in 1803. He writes:﻿—“We found plenty of goats and hogs. We saw some cats, and these three sorts of quadrupeds were the only animals we saw on the island.”

===Chinese===
美国船长阿马索·德拉诺（Amaso Delano）于 1803 年到访特林达迪。他写道：——「我们发现了大量山羊和野猪。我们还见到一些猫；这三种四足兽，便是我们在岛上见到的全部动物。」

===Original===
Possibly the land-crabs have gobbled all these up, for the only quadrupeds we came across were mice.

===Chinese===
兴许这些都被陆蟹吞吃干净了，因为我们此行遇到的四足兽只有老鼠。

===Original===
Having attained the summit of the island, the doctor and myself took a rest under the shade of the tree-ferns, while we partook of a frugal lunch of biscuits and rum, the indispensable pipes, of course, following.

===Chinese===
登上了全岛之巅，我和医生便在树蕨的荫翳下歇息，用了一顿俭省的午饭——面饼就朗姆酒，饭后照例抽起那两杆必不可少的烟斗。

## XI / 十一

===Original===
On the Road to Treasure Bay

===Chinese===
通往宝藏湾之路

===Original===
Having smoked our pipes we continued our journey. At first I was a very sanguine guide. I thought I should have no difficulty in recognising the ravine by which, nine years before, I had descended to the windward shore. But in this I was mistaken, for I found it extremely difficult to find my way to it again.

===Chinese===
抽完烟斗，我们继续赶路。起初我是个信心十足的向导。我以为，要认出九年前我循以下到向风岸去的那道裂谷，不会有什么难处。可在这一点上我错了：我发觉，要重新找到它，竟难乎其难。

===Original===
At any rate we were not now about to undergo the great toil, thirst, and danger that I had experienced during my former visit, for I at least knew some of the places to avoid, and this was a matter of importance. As we clambered along the edges of the mountains, looking for the pass, I was able to condemn at once as false passages several promising-looking routes, the vain trial of which had exhausted myself and my companions on my previous expedition.

===Chinese===
无论如何，这一回我们不至于再尝我上次来访时经历的那番辛劳、干渴与危险了，因为至少有哪些地方该避开，我是知道几处的，而这正是件要紧的事。我们沿着山缘手脚并用地攀行，一路寻找隘口；有几条看上去颇有希望的路，我可以当场断定为歧途——上一回探险，我们就是在这些路上白费气力，折腾得自己筋疲力尽。

===Original===
For instance, there was one long slope of volcanic debris of a ruddy colour which appeared from where we stood to join on to the green hills below and so to lead to the sandy beaches. The doctor was anxious to attempt this easy-looking way, but I knew the deceitful place too well of old. It tempts one further and further down, ever getting steeper, until one suddenly finds oneself at the edge of a frightful precipice, invisible from above, which compels one at great risk to retrace one’s painful steps to the heights.

===Chinese===
譬如，有一道长长的红褐色火山碎石坡，从我们站立之处望去，仿佛与下面的绿色冈峦连成一体，由此直通沙滩。医生很想试试这条看着轻省的路，可这骗人的地方我早年就领教透了。它诱着人越走越低，坡却越来越陡，直到人猛然发觉自己已站在一道骇人的绝壁边上——那壁从上面是望不见的——只好冒着大险，拖着酸楚的步子重新攀回高处。

===Original===
In the course of my first exploration we made so many false descents of these ravines and slopes, all terminating in precipices and driving us back again, that at last, finding no water, we were completely worn out and nearly perished of thirst. The heat is intense on Trinidad, especially at this season of the year, when the sun is vertical, and to climb these hot crags through the suffocating air is the most completely exhausting work I have ever undertaken. No other place within the tropics that I have visited has such an oppressive climate. I, therefore, determined to make no foolish experiments on this occasion, and not to attempt the descent until I was certain of my pass.

===Chinese===
头一回勘探时，我们在这些谷谷坡坡之间枉然下了一趟又一趟，趟趟尽头皆是绝壁，逼得我们原路折返；到后来寻不着水，人已困乏至极，险些渴死。特林达迪暑热逼人，尤其在一年的这个时节，太阳正当头顶；在令人窒息的空气里攀爬这些滚烫的危岩，是我平生做过的最耗人心力的活计。在我到过的热带地方里，没有一个的气候这般闷热难当。因此这一回我打定主意，再不做愚蠢的尝试，非到确有把握认准自己的隘口，决不贸然下坡。

===Original===
We crawled along the cliff-side for a long way, looking over at every point; but I could see nothing like my old ravine, and soon got fairly puzzled. At last we had followed the mountain ridges almost to the north end of the island, where the plateau of tree-ferns ceases, and where the mountains fall nearly perpendicularly into the sea, and culminate in needle-like peaks, affording no soil for vegetation of any description. So I knew that we had come too far and had passed the entrance to the ravine. We accordingly retraced our steps. We had now exhausted our bottle of water and were suffering from thirst. My old experience had taught me never, if possible, to be far from a stream while wandering over Trinidad. To toil among these arid rocks produces an insatiable thirst, and one’s strength fails if one is deprived of water even for a short time. Therefore as we saw below us a ravine that looked like a watercourse and which bore some resemblance to the one I was in search of, we decided to explore it. We lowered ourselves down from rock to rock for some way, and soon, to our delight, found a small issue of cool water. But this was not my ravine, for, on descending further, we came to the edge of one of the usual precipices, and we had to clamber up again.

===Chinese===
我们贴着崖边爬行了好长一段路，处处探头张望；可我望来望去，总望不见一点像旧日那道裂谷的影子，不久便当真犯了迷糊。到最后，我们顺着山脊几乎走到了岛的北端——那里树蕨高台已然到头，群山近乎笔直地插入海中，尽处的峰顶尖得像针，寸土不留，任何植物都无从着根。我便明白，我们走过了头，早已把裂谷的谷口抛在了身后。于是我们折返。这时瓶里的水已喝得精光，干渴正折磨着我们。往日的经验教会我：在特林达迪各地游走，只要办得到，千万不可远离溪流。在这些焦干的岩石间苦挣苦熬，会激起一种怎么也解不了的渴；断水哪怕只一小会儿，人的气力便要衰竭。因此，望见下面有一道谷，模样像条水道，又与我寻的那道有几分相像，我们便决意下去探查。我们从一块岩石缒到另一块岩石，下去了一程，不多时便欣喜地发现一小股清凉的泉水。然而这并不是我的那道谷：再往下走，便到了寻常那种绝壁的边上，我们只好又攀了上来。

===Original===
We attempted yet another ravine, which I did not recognise as *the* one, but which might prove to be it nevertheless, for I had to confess that I was quite at sea. This in time led us to a sloping shelf of rock overhanging another precipice. This shelf was extremely slippery, for the stream flowed over it in a thin film and it was covered with a short moss. This, too, exactly corresponds with a description in *Frank Mildmay*, that excellent guide to Trinidad, and what is said about the spot in that work may serve as a warning to any﻿—if such there ever be﻿—who may meditate a tour on this island. Two of Mildmay’s sailors had been lost while goat-hunting, so he sets forth in search of them. “I was some yards in advance of my companions,” he says, “and the dog a little distance from me, near the shelving part of a rock terminating in a precipice. The shelf I had to cross was about six or seven feet wide and ten or twelve long, with a very little inclined plane towards the precipice, so that I thought it perfectly safe. A small rill of water trickled down from the rock above it, and, losing itself among the moss and grass, fell over the precipice below, which, indeed, was of a frightful depth. This causeway was to all appearance safe, compared with many which we had passed, and I was just going to step upon it when my dog ran before me, jumped on the fatal pass﻿—his feet slipped from under him﻿—he fell and disappeared over the precipice! I started back﻿—I heard a heavy squelch and a howl; another fainter succeeded, and all was still. I advanced with the utmost caution to the edge of the precipice, where I discovered that the rill of water had nourished a short moss, close and smooth as velvet, and so slippery as not to admit of the lightest footstep; this accounted for the sudden disappearance and, as I concluded, the inevitable death of my dog.” Later on, far below, he found “the two dead bodies of our companions and that of my dog, all mangled in a shocking manner; both, it would appear, had attempted to cross the shelf in the same careless way which I was about to do when Providence interposed the dog in my behalf.” The adventures of Frank Mildmay and his crew on Trinidad are recorded with such realism and with﻿—as I have before said﻿—such accuracy of local colouring, that I suspect Captain Marryat in this portion of his work is recounting his personal experiences.

===Chinese===
我们又试了一道裂谷。我说不清它究竟是不是那一条，但它说不定偏偏就是，因为我不得不承认，自己已经全然摸不着头脑了。这谷到头来把我们引到一方斜斜的石台上，台外又临着一道绝壁。石台滑得厉害，因为溪水在上面敷着薄薄一层流过，台上又生着短短的青苔。这一节恰与《弗兰克·米尔徳梅》——那部特林达迪绝好的指南——里的一段描写吻合；书中关于此处的记述，对任何当真想上这岛游历的人——倘若世上果真有这样的人——都可作一份鉴戒。米尔徳梅手下有两名水手猎山羊时走失了，他便动身去寻。「我走在同伴们前头几码，」他写道，「狗离我不远，在一方岩石的斜台近旁，那石台尽头便是绝壁。我要横穿的这道石台约六七英尺宽、十一二英尺长，朝绝壁那一面稍稍倾斜，因此我以为它万无一失。一缕细流从上方的岩石渗下来，隐没于苔藓与草间，再从下面的绝壁上跌落下去；那绝壁委实深得骇人。与我们已经走过的许多石台相比，这道路面上看去是安全的；我正要踏上去，我的狗抢在前面跑过，跳上了那条夺命通道——它脚下一滑——跌了下去，翻过绝壁没了踪影！我猛地缩回——听见一声沉闷的扑响和一声哀嚎；接着又是一声，弱了些，随后万籁俱寂。我极其小心地挪到绝壁边缘，才发现那缕细流滋养出一层短苔，密实平滑如天鹅绒，滑得连最轻的脚步也站不住；这一下才解释了它何以骤然消失，也正如我所推断的，解释了我的狗不可避免的死。」随后，在下方深处，他找到了「我们两名同伴的尸体和我那条狗的尸体，全都残破得骇人；看来两人都是想以我方才险些采用的同样漫不经心的方式横穿石台，而那时，是天意把狗遣来替我挡了灾」。弗兰克·米尔徳梅和他一伙人在特林达迪的历险，写得如此逼真，又——如我先前所说——带着如此精确的地方色彩，以致我疑心马里亚特船长在这一部分写的正是他本人的亲身经历。

===Original===
So, foiled once again, we reascended the ravine and walked along the edge of the mountains, till we came to a projecting rock that commanded an extensive view over the cliffs. Here we sat down and discussed the problem before us. I assured the doctor that my ravine was certainly close to us somewhere, but that I altogether failed to identify it among the ravines before us, though I carried in my mind’s eye a very vivid picture of its appearance.

===Chinese===
于是我们又落了空，重新爬上谷来，沿着山缘走，直到一处凸出的岩石跟前——从那里可以纵览崖下一大片景象。我们坐下来，商量眼前的难题。我向医生担保，我那道裂谷必定就在近旁某处，只是眼前这一片谷壑之中，我怎么也认不出它来，虽然它的模样，在我心目中还留着极鲜明的一幅图景。

===Original===
“Perhaps it has disappeared,” suggested the doctor. This seemed scarcely possible, but it might, I acknowledged, have been so changed by landslips as to be unrecognisable.

===Chinese===
「兴许它消失了。」医生提示道。这看上去几乎不可能，但我承认，它或许已被滑坡改得面目全非，以致认不出了。

===Original===
Being people of logical mind, we reasoned that, if the ravine still existed, we ought now to discover it without any difficulty by a simple process of elimination. There was only a limited number of even possible-looking ways down the precipices. Of these we had now tried two in vain. Again, there were several others which I remembered well to have attempted at the time of my previous visit and to have found impracticable. It followed that we had now to confine our attention to any remaining possible routes, and of these there could be very few.

===Chinese===
我们都是有逻辑头脑的人，一番推理下来：那道裂谷倘若还在，如今只须用简单的排除法，便该毫不费力地把它找出来。看上去哪怕仅有一线可能下崖的路，总数本来有限。这其中，我们已经白白试了两条。再者，还有几条我记得清楚，上次来访时试过，明知走不通。由此推来，我们如今只须把注意力集中在余下尚有可能的几条路上，而这为数必然很少。

===Original===
Indeed, after a careful survey along the edge of the cliffs, we found that there was but one such way left to us, and that looked very ugly. Everywhere else were precipices that could obviously only be descended by a means of progression more rapid than we cared to undertake.

===Chinese===
果然，沿着崖缘仔细踏勘一遍之后，我们发现这样的路只剩一条，而且模样凶险。其余各处皆是绝壁，显然，除非采用某种比我们乐意尝试的更为迅捷的行进方式，否则绝无可能下去。

===Original===
This way seemed as if it might afford a passage to the beach, but it was not a ravine at all. The mountain on which we stood had fallen away, leaving a precipitous step some fifty or sixty feet in height, and from this step there sloped down to a depth, I should say, of quite 1,500 feet a great landslip of broken rocks, the debris of the fallen mountain. This landslip appeared to have taken place not long since. It was composed of rocks of all sizes and shapes, almost coal black, piled one on the other at so steep an angle that it was extraordinary how the mass held together and did not topple over. It was indeed in places more like an artificial wall of rough stones on a gigantic scale than a landslip.

===Chinese===
这条路看模样倒也许能通到滩头，但它根本算不上什么裂谷。我们脚下所立的整面山体塌了下去，留下一道五六十英尺高的陡坎；从这道坎往下，斜铺着一大片破岩构成的滑坡——崩塌山体的碎屑——依我看，深达足有 1,500 英尺。这场滑坡看去发生未久。构成它的岩石大大小小、奇形怪状，近乎炭黑，一块摞着一块，堆出的坡角陡得出奇，整堆东西居然拢得住、不曾倾倒，实在叫人称奇。有些地方，它与其说是滑坡，不如说更像一道放大到极致的毛石砌墙。

===Original===
The pass I was searching for was utterly unlike this. I remembered well that I had found a ravine extending from the mountain top to the beach, which I described in my narrative as “a gloomy gorge with sides formed of black rocks piled on each other in chaotic masses, with a small stream trickling into it.” We had experienced little difficulty in ascending or descending it. Before us were now a sufficiency “of black rocks piled on each other in chaotic masses,” but no signs of a ravine or stream.

===Chinese===
我寻的那条通路与此全然不同。我记得清楚，当年我找到的是一道从山顶直通滩头的裂谷，我在自己的记行文字里曾把它描写成「一道阴森的峡谷，两岸是乱纷纷堆叠起来的黑岩，一脉细流涓涓注入其间」。上下这道谷，我们当年没费什么力气。如今眼前倒尽有「乱纷纷堆叠起来的黑岩」，却不见半点裂谷或溪流的踪影。

===Original===
It did not look a tempting route, but we could see nothing else, so decided to try it. The descent was anything but easy and was certainly rather trying to the nerves. To begin with, the descent of the precipitous step I have mentioned was a very creepy business. Having accomplished this without accident, we clambered down the giant staircase of black rocks the best way we could, and also with as much speed as was consistent with safety; for the sun was low, the sudden tropical night would soon be on us, and as it would be, of course, impossible to proceed in the dark, we should be compelled to camp out in this very uncomfortable place if we did not hurry on.

===Chinese===
这条路看着并不诱人，但除它之外再无他途，我们便决意一试。下行的路决谈不上轻巧，而且着实考验神经。首先，下我方才提到的那道陡坎，就是一桩叫人头皮发麻的营生。这一关安然闯过之后，我们便在黑岩砌成的巨大阶梯上，拣着最好的走法往下攀，同时在安全许可之内尽量求快；因为太阳已经低了，热带的黑夜说至即至，而黑地里自然寸步难行，若不赶紧前行，我们就只好在这极不舒服的地方露宿了。

===Original===
We at last reached the foot of the landslip, and were on the green down we had seen from above, and which slopes gently to the beach. All our difficulties were over.

===Chinese===
我们终于下到滑坡脚下，踏上了从上面望见的那片绿色草冈，它缓缓倾斜，直伸向滩头。我们的种种难处就此过去了。

===Original===
These slopes on the windward side of Trinidad are overgrown chiefly with a sturdy species of bean. This plant creeps along the ground, throwing out long tough tendrils, whose mission it evidently is to climb up something for support; but in this they are generally unsuccessful, for nearly all the dead trees have been blown down on this windswept corner of the island. A few trees are still standing, and these are overgrown with clinging creepers more lucky than the rest. The scene reminded me of countries I had visited where there are ten women to one man and where, consequently, the male is properly appreciated and made much of, while thousands of luckless old maids vegetate hopelessly with no one to cling to. When I imparted this simile to the doctor he implored me not to be sentimental.

===Chinese===
特林达迪向风一侧的这些山坡，主要长满了一种粗壮的豆类植物。它贴着地面蔓生，抽出又长又韧的卷须，其天命显然是要攀住些什么作为依傍；可这件事它们多半办不成，因为在这岛上迎风的角落，枯树几乎全被吹倒了。尚有几棵树兀自站着，便缠满了比同类走运的攀缘藤蔓。眼前这景象令我想起我到过的一些国度：那里男女之比是女十男一，于是男子便得到应有的赏识与恩宠，而千百个倒霉的老姑娘无人可依，只能无望地枯守到老。我把这个比方说给医生听，他恳求我不要自作多情。

===Original===
The flowers of this bean are pink, and the pods are as large as broad beans. These the doctor at once pronounced to be edible, for, as he explained to me, none of these Leguminosae are poisonous. This was a good thing to know, for they grow so thickly on these shores that we could have collected any quantity we pleased during our stay on Trinidad; and with these, the fish, the turtle, the birds and their eggs, all of which are procurable here without any difficulty, it would be possible for men left on this island to ward off starvation for any length of time.

===Chinese===
这种豆开粉红色的花，豆荚有蚕豆那么大。医生当即断言这些可以入口，因为——按他给我的解释——这类豆科（Leguminosae）植物没有一种是有毒的。这是件值得知道的事：这些豆在沿岸长得极密，我们居留特林达迪期间想要多少便可采多少；有了它们，再加上鱼、海龟、海鸟和鸟蛋——这一切在此都唾手可得——就算有人被留在岛上，也尽可以无限期地免于饿死。

===Original===
When I speak of the slopes we were now on as downs, the reader must not conjure up a picture of the grassy downs of the English coast, pleasant under foot and easy to travel on. To drag one’s feet over the downs of Trinidad is a very weary business. There are large rocks and deep pits everywhere. One’s progress is impeded by the extreme softness of the soil, into which one’s feet sink deeply, and this is made still worse by the burrows of the land-crabs, while the roots of the tall grasses and the trailing tendrils of the beans try to trip one up at every step.

===Chinese===
我把我们此刻所走的这些山坡叫作草冈，读者切莫由此在脑中描出一幅英国海岸草冈的图景——那里脚下舒坦，行走轻易。在特林达迪的草冈上拖步而行，是件极累人的事。巨石深坑处处皆是。土质松软之极，双脚深深陷进去，行进大受其阻；陆蟹打的洞又使这情形雪上加霜，高草的根和豆蔓拖地的卷须还步步都想绊你一跤。

===Original===
Here, to our relief, we found water again. At the foot of the landslip a deep gully opened out which clove the down to the edge of the shore. At the bottom of this a little stream flowed for a short distance, being absorbed by the thirsty soil long before it could reach the sands below.

===Chinese===
在这里我们又找到了水，甚是宽慰。滑坡脚下岔开一道深深的沟壑，把草冈直劈到岸边。沟底一线小溪流了短短一程，早在够到下面的沙滩之前，就被干渴的泥土吸尽了。

===Original===
In order to avoid the entangling vegetation we walked down this gully, and an exceedingly unpleasant place we found it. For here an incredible number of large fluffy white birds, a sort of gannet, were sitting on their nests with their young. They covered the rocks and the branches of the dead trees. They attacked us savagely whenever we came within reach of them, and the whole of the hot narrow gorge stank most offensively of the rotten fish they had strewed about. The different species of birds occupy different portions of this island, and this ravine is the chief haunt of this particular disagreeable tribe.

===Chinese===
为了避开缠人的草木，我们顺着这道沟往下走，却发现这是个极不痛快的地方。原来这里有数目多得难以置信的白色大鸟，绒毛蓬松，是一种塘鹅，正伏在巢中孵育幼雏。它们密密地盖满岩石和枯树的枝桠。我们一走进它们够得着的地方，它们便凶猛地攻击我们；整条又热又窄的峡谷里，弥漫着它们抛撒四下的烂鱼，臭得叫人掩鼻。不同的鸟种分占此岛的不同地段，而这道谷，正是这个特别讨人嫌的族类聚居的老巢。

===Original===
The whole scene now seemed strangely familiar to me﻿—the ravine, the black rocks, the crowds of brooding white birds﻿—and when at last we came to what appeared to be an old road of piled-up stones crossing the gully I stood still and cried in astonishment: “Why, doctor, this is my ravine after all! I remember this place well!”

===Chinese===
此刻整个景象令我莫名地眼熟——这道谷，这些黑岩，这一群群伏巢的白鸟——待到我们终于走到一处像是垒石铺成的旧路、横跨沟壑的地方，我站定了，惊呼起来：「哎呀，医生，这到底还是我那道谷！这地方我记得清清楚楚！」

===Original===
Then I looked behind me at the mountain we had descended, and I began to understand how it was that I had been unable to find out my old route. As I have explained, the ravine I had travelled down nine years before extended from the plateau of tree-ferns to the shore. But since then a gigantic landslip had evidently taken place. The mountainside had fallen away, and millions and millions of tons of rocks had rolled below, entirely filling up the ravine and destroying all traces of it, until far down, where it appeared again on the downs beyond the limit of the landslip.

===Chinese===
这时我回身仰望我们下来的那座山，才渐渐明白，我何以寻不见自己的旧路。如前所述，九年前我下去的那道裂谷，是从树蕨高台一直通到岸边的。可自那以后，这里显然发生过一场天崩地陷般的巨型滑坡。整面山坡垮了下去，千千万万吨巨石滚落下来，把那道谷整个填平，抹去了它的一切痕迹，直到下方远处，它才在滑坡界线之外的草冈上重新露头。

===Original===
This was one among other instances I can mention showing that enormous changes have taken place on this island even in the course of the last nine years. When this terrific fall of rocks occurred, it would have been a wonderful sight to one gazing at it in safety from the sea, and the noise of it must have made itself heard for many leagues around. It has certainly converted what was once a comparatively easy and perfectly safe road from the mountaintops to the windward shore into an extremely difficult and dangerous one. So much so that the doctor and myself saw at once that it would be useless to establish a depot of stores at the pier, as it would be out of the question to lead the members of the expedition up such a perilous place as this. It was absolutely certain that lives would be lost if this pass were often attempted. No skilful mountaineering would avail against the treacherous rottenness of the precipitous step which surmounts the landslip, and which did not exist of old. There is no certain foothold anywhere upon its face, and we looked forward with no pleasurable anticipation to our enforced return by this way on the morrow.

===Chinese===
这是我可以举出的例证之一，说明单是最近这九年之间，此岛便已发生了天翻地覆的巨变。那场骇人的崩岩倾泻之时，若有人从海上安然遥望，必是一大奇观；其声势之隆，周围许多里格之外想必都听得见。它确凿无疑地把昔日一条自山顶下向风岸的较为平易、十分安全的路，变成了极难走、极凶险的一条。凶险到这个地步，我和医生当下便看出：在天然码头设立给养储存点已属徒劳，因为要率领探险队的队员攀上这样一处险地，根本无从谈起。这条道若常常有人涉足，断然要出人命。那道压在滑坡顶上的陡坎是旧日所无的，其朽坏暗藏杀机，任凭什么高明的登山本领也对付不了。陡坎的坡面上没有任何一处站得牢的落脚点；想到明天还非得原路由此返回，我们实在提不起半点愉快的期待。

===Original===
The birds’ eggs lay on every stone in this valley. We tasted some of them, but the flavour bore too much resemblance to the stench of rotten fish around us to be altogether pleasing.

===Chinese===
这谷里的石头上到处搁着鸟蛋。我们尝了几枚，可那滋味与四下烂鱼的恶臭太相像了，实在谈不上可口。

===Original===
The bank of stones which I had recognised in the ravine was of far too regular formation to be otherwise than the work of men’s hands.

===Chinese===
我在谷中认出的那道石堤，砌得过于规整，除了出自人手，不可能是别的来历。

===Original===
Some hundreds of years ago, the Portuguese had a penal settlement on this side of Trinidad, and this, no doubt, was what remained of one of their roads. Some weeks later, I explored the ruins of this settlement, which is a short distance to the north of this gully. I will describe it when I come to that portion of my narrative.

===Chinese===
数百年前，葡萄牙人曾在特林达迪的这一面设有流放犯的屯居地；这无疑便是他们某条道路的遗存。数周之后，我踏勘了这处屯居地的废墟，它在这道沟壑以北不远的地方。待写到记行的那一段时，我再细细描述。

===Original===
Before we came to the spot where the stream soaks into the earth we filled our bottle with water; then we walked down to the sandy beach, reaching it just before it became too dark to see our way. We were not long in selecting our camp. There was a large rock on the sands above high-water mark, whose hollow side afforded good shelter from wind and rain. In front of this, we lit a fire of the wreckwood, of which there was no lack round us, and after a supper of roasted *ch’arki* and biscuit, we proceeded to make ourselves comfortable over our pipes and rum. We were tired, and would have slept very soundly with the sound of the surf on the reefs as our lullaby, had it not been for the land-crabs, which would not let us alone, but pulled our hair or nipped our necks as soon as we began to doze off.

===Chinese===
走到溪水渗进土里的地方之前，我们先把水瓶灌满；随后下到沙滩，赶在天黑得看不见路之前到了那里。选营地没费多少工夫。高潮线以上的沙地上立着一块大岩石，凹进的一面正好遮风避雨。我们在岩前生起一堆火，烧的是失事船的漂木——四下里这东西有的是——晚饭吃了烤恰尔基风干牛肉和硬面饼，饭后便就着烟斗和朗姆酒，舒舒服服地安顿下来。我们实在乏了，本可伴着礁石上涛声的摇篮曲酣然沉睡，奈何陆蟹不肯放过我们，我们刚一迷糊，它们便来扯头发、掐脖子。

===Original===
At last their conduct became unbearable, and our patience worn out, so we got up, seized two sticks, and slaughtered some fifty of them. Then we had a little rest, for the others left us alone for a while and devoured their dead brethren, making a merry crackling noise all round us, as they pulled the joints asunder and opened the shells. It was, as the doctor remarked, like the sound of many lobster suppers going on together at Scott’s.

===Chinese===
到后来它们的行径实在叫人忍无可忍，我们的耐性也磨光了，便一跃而起，操起两根木棍，结果了大约五十只。此后我们才得了片刻安宁，因为其余的蟹暂且丢下我们不管，专心吞吃起它们的死同类来；它们把蟹腿扯下、把壳掰开，四下里响成一片欢快的咔嚓声。正如医生所说，这就像斯科茨餐馆（Scott’s）里许多顿龙虾晚宴同时在开动。

===Original===
At daybreak (Nov. 22) we started for Southwest Bay. We had drunk all our water, and so were anxious to reach the bay, explore it, and be back to our stream as quickly as possible. While making this same journey nine years before, I had found no signs of fresh water between this and South Point. The streams that flow from the mountaintops are absorbed far up by the slopes of debris and never reach the shore. Mr. A﻿—﻿— did discover a small, but uncertain, supply near his camp at the head of Southwest Bay, but we felt that we could not rely on this, and that the issue in the ravine above us, which we had left on the previous evening, was the only one we could fall back upon with certainty on the whole weather shore of the island.

===Chinese===
破晓时分（11 月 22 日），我们动身前往西南湾。水已喝尽，我们都急着赶到湾边，踏勘一番，尽快回到我们的溪水那里去。九年前走这同一段路时，从这里到南角之间，我没有发现丝毫淡水的迹象。从山顶流下的溪水，早早就被碎石坡吸干，从来不曾流到岸边。A——先生确曾在西南湾湾头他营地附近找到过一小股水源，但时有时无；我们却觉得这一点靠不住——全岛整个向风岸上，我们所能确凿仰仗的，只有前一晚留在身后那道谷里的泉水。

===Original===
We walked along the sandy beach, with the mountains towering to the right of us and the ocean swell breaking heavily on the reefs to our left. The beach was covered with wreckage﻿—planks, barrels, spars, timbers of vessels with the corroded iron bolts still sticking in them﻿—a melancholy spectacle; but I was unable to find one particular wreck which I had seen here nine years before﻿—the complete framework of a vessel partly buried in the sands, into which I had thought it might be worth while for our party now to dig, as some valuables might be lying in her hold. Either the sea had broken up or the sands had completely covered this wreck since my last visit.

===Chinese===
我们沿着沙滩前行，右侧群山巍然耸峙，左侧大洋的涌浪在礁石上重重拍碎。滩上覆满失事船只的残骸——木板、木桶、桅桁、船材，锈蚀的铁螺栓还钉在上面——一派凄凉景象；但九年前我在这里见过的一处特别的残骸，这回却怎么也找不到了——那是一艘船的完整骨架，半埋在沙里；我曾想着，如今让我们这一队人来挖上一挖倒也值得，说不定船舱里还躺着些什么值钱的东西。自上次来访，那残骸不是被大海打散了，便是被流沙整个埋没了。

===Original===
We found traces of turtle on the sands, and we saw that the pools of clear water left by the tide were full of fish, while sea-crabs scampered over the rocks in quantities. The beans, too, grew in profusion on the downs above the beach, so there was plenty of food all round us, and, if there had only been fresh water, we could have made ourselves very comfortable here. There were, of course, plenty of land-crabs everywhere, but one would have to be hard driven to eat these ugly brutes.

===Chinese===
我们在沙滩上发现了海龟的痕迹，又见潮水退后留下的清水洼里满是游鱼，海蟹则成群地在礁石间窜来窜去。滩上的草冈也盛长着那种豆子，所以我们四周食物俯拾即是；只要再有淡水，我们在此本可以过得十分惬意。陆蟹当然遍地都是，不过真要吃这些丑家伙，人总得被逼到山穷水尽的地步才行。

===Original===
At last we came to a promontory of rock jutting out into the sea. We climbed up this without difficulty, and descended the other side by a steep slope of soft white sand.

===Chinese===
最后我们来到一座探入海中的石岬跟前。我们毫不费力地爬上岬顶，又顺着对面一道松软白沙的陡坡下到另一侧。

===Original===
From here we could see before us the Sugarloaf and Noah’s Ark. The former mountain, as its name implies, is of conical shape﻿—a stupendous mass, apparently of grey granite, whose summit is about 1,500 feet above the sea, and which on one side is very nearly perpendicular. Noah’s Ark (South Point on the Admiralty chart) was so named by myself at the time of my former visit, in consequence of its resemblance both in shape and colour to the favourite toy of my childhood. It is of oblong form, with perpendicular sides and with a top exactly like the roof of a house. It is formed of volcanic rock of a peculiar reddish colour, and is about 800 feet in height. These two strangely-shaped mountains are joined together by an apparently inaccessible ridge composed chiefly of the red detritus from Noah’s Ark.

===Chinese===
从这里，糖面包山和「诺亚方舟」（Noah’s Ark）都展现在我们眼前。前一座山，顾名思义，作圆锥之形——一大块惊人体量的巨物，看似灰色花岗岩，峰顶高出海面约 1,500 英尺，一面几乎笔直陡立。「诺亚方舟」是我上次来访时亲自给它起的名，即海军部海图上的南角，因为它无论形状还是颜色，都像极了我童年心爱的那件玩具。它呈长方形，四壁垂直，顶上一平，恰似一座房子的屋顶。它由一种特殊红褐色的火山岩构成，高约 800 英尺。这两座奇形怪状的山由一道看去无法攀登的山脊相连，那山脊主要由「诺亚方舟」崩落的红色碎屑构成。

===Original===
Our destination, Southwest Bay, is bounded on its east side by these mountains; it was, therefore, necessary for us now, being south of East Point, to cross the intervening heights.

===Chinese===
我们的目的地西南湾，东侧便以这几座山为界；因此，我们此刻既已在东角以南，便必须翻越横亘其间的高地。

===Original===
The only pass I knew was just under the Sugarloaf. This we used generally to speak of as the Sugarloaf Col, so as to distinguish it from another pass which we afterwards discovered. Sugarloaf Col is the gap which divides the Sugarloaf from a jagged peak to the north of it, and which, in its turn, is continued by the steep downs which lie to the back of Southwest Bay.

===Chinese===
我所知道的唯一隘口就在糖面包山脚下。我们平时通常把它叫作「糖面包山隘」（Sugarloaf Col），以便与后来发现的另一条通道相区别。糖面包山隘是糖面包山与其北侧一座嶙峋尖峰之间的垭口，而垭口往下，又接上躺在西南湾背后的一片陡峭草冈。

===Original===
We crossed the sands, and then a small plain covered with a variety of bushes, which brought us to the foot of the Col. This gap is formed of rocks piled on one another, and is not difficult to surmount.

===Chinese===
我们穿过沙滩，又走过一片生满各色灌木的小平原，便到了山隘脚下。这道隘口由乱石层层垒成，翻越并不艰难。

===Original===
We reached the summit of it and then, looking down on the other side, we beheld, lying at our feet, Treasure Bay at last.

===Chinese===
我们登上隘口顶巅，朝另一面俯望——宝藏湾终于躺在我们的脚下。

## XII / 十二

===Original===
We Explore the Ravine

===Chinese===
我们踏勘裂谷

===Original===
As we stood on the Col, the steep wall of the Sugarloaf rising to the left of us, the view over Southwest Bay was exceedingly fine. The bay is of semicircular form, with a distance of about a mile and a half from point to point. Broad sands, with green downs behind them, border the central portion; but it is bounded by steep bare mountains on either side: on the east side by Noah's Ark, the Sugarloaf and the peaks beyond; and on the west side by the rugged promontories and islands which divide it from South Bay. In contrast to the savage cliffs that shut them in, the sands and downs in the middle of the bay present a very pleasing and fertile appearance, especially when seen from the sea, conveying the idea that this is a far more agreeable spot to live on than proves to be the case after a closer examination.

===Chinese===
我们站在山隘上，左侧糖面包山的陡壁巍然拔起，俯瞰西南湾的景致极佳。这湾作半圆形，自此角至彼角相距约一英里半。宽宽的沙滩背后衬着绿色草冈，镶在湾的中段；两侧却尽是陡峭光秃的群山：东侧是「诺亚方舟」、糖面包山和更远处的诸峰，西侧则是把它与南湾隔开的嶙峋海岬与岛屿。与圈锁着它们的荒莽峭崖相对照，湾心的沙滩与草冈显得颇为宜人丰饶，从海上望去尤其如此，令人觉得这里是个宜居得多的去处——待到细加查验，方知实情并非如此。

===Original===
From the Col we could look right down on the bay, and, as the water was very clear, we were able to distinguish all the dangers below the surface, as well as those above. It was, no doubt, from here that the pirate captain made his survey.

===Chinese===
从山隘上我们可以径直俯视整片湾，海水又极清澈，水面上的险处、水面下的暗险，我们全都分辨得一清二楚。海盗船长当年测量这一带，无疑正是从这处观望的。

===Original===
We saw that an islet, unmarked on any chart, rose in the middle of the bay, while a reef of rocks, apparently coral, extended right round the bay, parallel to the beach, and at a short distance from it. Some of these rocks were above the surface of the water, some just below, and others—the most dangerous—further down, so that it was only occasionally that the sea broke upon them. The pirate in his confession had spoken of a channel he had discovered through this reef, situated under the Sugarloaf, at the eastern extremity of the bay. We now saw that it existed there exactly as he had described it—a broad opening in the line of rocks, through which a boat could be pulled, and beached on the sands.

===Chinese===
我们看见湾心凸起一座小岛，任何海图上都未标绘；还有一列礁石——看去是珊瑚礁——顺着湾绕了整整一圈，与海滩平行，相距不远。这些礁石有的露出水面，有的刚没在水面之下，还有的——最凶险的——沉得更深，只有偶尔才有海浪砸在上面。海盗在供词里说过，他在这条礁脉中发现了一道水道，位置在糖面包山之下、湾的东端。此刻我们看见它果真就在那里，与他描述的分毫不差——礁石线上豁开一道宽口，小艇可以摇着进去，径直靠上沙滩。

===Original===
But still, it was an awkward place, and it would be impossible to land there on such a day as this was, for immense rollers were sweeping up the shore which would have almost certainly dashed any boat to pieces that ventured among them. We were, however, very satisfied with the success of our expedition so far. We had discovered and taken bearings of the channel, and we knew how to pilot a boat through it, when the weather should be favourable. Our next duty was to descend into the bay and identify the place where the treasure was supposed to be hidden.

===Chinese===
不过，这终究是处难弄的地方，像今天这样的天气根本无从登岸，因为巨大的滚浪一排排卷上滩来，哪条船胆敢闯入其中，几乎必被砸得粉碎。然而对迄今的收获，我们已颇为满意。水道既已寻见并测得方位，待天气转好，如何引一条小艇穿行其间，我们心中有数。下一步的职责，便是下到湾里，认明那处据信藏宝的所在。

===Original===
It was not long before we had discovered what we considered to be the right spot.

===Chinese===
不多时，我们便发现了自认为正确的那处地点。

===Original===
The pirate had described a small gully in the middle of this bay, at the foot of which he and his men had erected three cairns, which should serve as landmarks to those who had the clue, and point the way to the treasure.

===Chinese===
海盗曾描述，这湾的中部有一道小沟壑，他和他手下的人在沟脚垒起三座石冢，好给握有线索的人充作路标，指引通往宝藏的路。

===Original===
Mr. P——, and, after him, Mr. A——, had found this gully and the three cairns, just as they had been described. Mr. A——, either for the purpose of putting others off the scent, or in order to discover if anything had been concealed beneath them, blew up these cairns with gunpowder and dug into them, so that now we could only see traces of one of them. He had, however, communicated to me what he understood to be their signification, and how he had been led by them to the first bend in the ravine, at which spot the plunder had been buried under a hollow rock.

===Chinese===
P——先生，以及随后的A——先生，都找到过这道沟壑和那三座石冢，与所描述的毫厘不爽。A——先生或是为了绝了别人的念想，或是想查明石冢底下是否藏过东西，用火药把石冢炸开又掘了几掘，以致如今我们只能看出其中一座的残迹。好在他已把自己领会的石冢含义相告，也讲过它们如何引他走到裂谷的第一道弯——赃物便埋在那处地方一块中空的岩石底下。

===Original===
We walked up the ravine till we came to a bend, and here, as we had expected, we saw what appeared to be a landslip of red earth, filling up the corner of it, blocking up the mouth of any cave that might exist there, even as Mr. P—— and Mr. A—— had described. And here before us lay a small trench, with a broken earthenware water jar and the remains of a wheelbarrow lying in it—all that remained to show where Mr. A—— had carried on his not very extensive works.

===Chinese===
我们沿谷上行，来到一道弯处；在这里，正如我们所料，见到一片像是红土滑坡的堆积，填满了谷弯的角落，把那里可能存在的任何洞穴的洞口都堵了个严实——与P——先生和A——先生描述的正相吻合。眼前还横着一道小沟，沟里躺着一只破陶水罐和一辆手推车的残骸——这是仅存的一切，标示着A——先生当年施工之地；他那工程实在算不得浩大。

===Original===
This, therefore, was the spot we had crossed the Atlantic to find. We stood and looked at it in silence for a while. "What do you think of it?" asked the doctor at last.

===Chinese===
于是，这便是我们横渡大西洋前来寻找的地方了。我们站在那里，默然望了半晌。「你觉得这里如何？」医生终于问道。

===Original===
It was not an easy question to reply to, for I did not quite know myself what to think of it. I had pictured to myself a very different place. I saw that our work would in one respect be more difficult than I had anticipated, in another respect far more easy. For this landslip was not nearly so extensive as I had understood it to be, and the slopes of the ravine were not of such a character as to render our operations dangerous, or to necessitate any timbering of our shafts or trenches. But, on the other hand, there was a want of definiteness that was disappointing. There were no really sharp bends in the ravine, and there were several landslips. It was impossible to be quite certain of what was meant by "the first bend;" for there were bends of so insignificant a character that they might easily be overlooked; and we had no knowledge of the number of paces from the cairns to the cavern. Therefore, should we fail to find the treasure at the spot where Mr. A—— commenced to dig, it would be necessary for us to clear the landslip off the face of the cliff for some considerable distance.

===Chinese===
这问题不好回答，因为我自己也不大清楚该作何想。我想象中的本是另一副模样。我看出来，我们的工程在某一方面将比预想的更难，在另一方面又远为容易。这片滑坡远不像我所了解的那样广大，谷坡的性态也不至于让我们的作业有危险，竖井和沟槽都用不着支坑木。但另一方面，这里缺一种确定性，令人失望。谷中没有当真峻急的转弯，滑坡倒有好几处。「第一道弯」究竟何指，根本无从确定：有的弯细小得那么不起眼，稍一疏忽便会放过；从石冢到洞窟该走多少步，我们也一无所知。因此，倘若在A——先生动土之处寻不到宝藏，我们就得把崖面上的滑坡清开好长一段。

===Original===
Having inspected the scene of Mr. A——'s operations, we set out to explore the ravine carefully, and, bearing in mind what we knew of the pirate's original instructions, we endeavoured to reason out whether this or some other neighbouring bend was the most likely spot. The treasure was lying, or had been lying, very close to us somewhere; of that I felt confident at the time, and I have had no reason for altering my opinion since.

===Chinese===
察看过A——先生作业的现场，我们便着手细细踏勘这道谷，一面记着我们所知的海盗原始指令，一面推究究竟这道弯，还是近旁哪一道别的弯，最有可能藏物。宝藏当时正埋着，或曾埋着，就在离我们很近的某处；这一点我当时深信不疑，此后也没有理由更改看法。

===Original===
First, we went down the ravine again, and when we reached the bottom of it, where it opens out upon the back of the beach, we observed, what had escaped our notice at first, an extensive excavation in the hard soil—which is not so encumbered with boulders here as it is higher up—a cutting so regular in form and with such perpendicular sides that it was difficult to imagine that it had not been the work of men's hands. This was certainly not one of Mr. A——'s trenches; for to have removed such a quantity of earth and stones would have occupied such a party as he had with him for six months at least.

===Chinese===
我们先重新下到谷底。谷底在滩背后豁然开阔，就在那里，我们注意到一处起先失于留意的大坑——掘在坚硬的泥土里；这一带的土不像上游那样堆满巨石——坑形齐整，四壁垂直，很难想象并非出自人手。这断然不是A——先生挖的沟：要起走这许多土石，凭他随行那班人手，至少也得六个月。

===Original===
Was it possible that the American, or some other adventurer, had been here before us and carried away the treasure? We could find no marks of tools or other traces of man in or near this trench, so it was impossible to decide whether it was artificial or natural. Some of us afterwards came to the conclusion that it was most probably the latter, for we came across other cuttings, somewhat similar to this, in other portions of the ravine, which had evidently been produced by the action of water.

===Chinese===
莫非那美国人，或是别的什么冒险家，已抢在我们之前来过，把宝藏运走了？我们在这坑里坑外都寻不到工具的凿痕或其他人迹，因而无从断定它是人力所成还是天然生成。后来我们中有人得出结论：多半是后者，因为我们在谷中别处也遇到过几道与此略似的沟槽，显见是水流冲刷出来的。

===Original===
Next we went up the gully beyond Mr. A——'s trench, in the hopes of finding water, of which we were beginning to feel the want. There was no running stream here, though it was evident from its formation that the ravine was swept by a mighty torrent after heavy rains. The water that drained into it from the overhanging mountain was soaked up by the loose red soil that lay between the boulders.

===Chinese===
接着，我们越过A——先生的沟，往谷的上段走去，指望寻到水——渴意已开始袭来。这里没有奔流的溪水，不过从谷的形貌看，每逢大雨之后必有一股洪流席卷此谷，这是显而易见的。从头顶山上泄进谷里的水，全被巨石之间松散的红土吸尽了。

===Original===
But at last we came to a little hollow at the foot of a rocky step, where was a tiny pool of tepid and muddy water. However, this was all we required, for we could now afford time to survey the scene of our operations more thoroughly, instead of hurrying back, driven by thirst, to our distant watercourse.

===Chinese===
但我们终于来到一道石阶脚下的小洼地，那里存着一小汪温吞浑浊的水。然而这已尽够我们所用，因为如今可以从容不迫，把作业的现场踏勘个透彻，而不必再被干渴驱赶着，匆匆赶回远处那道溪流去。

===Original===
Between the hills and the beach, close to the mouth of the ravine, there is a sort of plateau of sand and stones, and it was evidently on this that Mr. A—— had pitched his camp, for here we came across his tent poles, the remains of wheelbarrows, and some empty meat-tins.

===Chinese===
群山与海滩之间、紧挨谷口的地方，有一片沙石垒成的小台地；A——先生的营地显然就扎在这里，因为我们在这里撞见了他留下的帐篷杆、手推车的残骸，还有几只空的肉罐头。

===Original===
We walked down to the eastern beach, where the landing was, opposite the channel between the coral rocks. The sands here sloped steeply into deepish water; it was, apparently, an excellent place for beaching a boat when the state of the weather should allow. Though it was a windless day the ocean swell was high, and it was a grand sight to see the great green rollers sweep majestically up till they were close to the beach, and then curl over and break in showers of sparkling spray. While we stood there admiring the scene, we saw a curious sight. A roller was travelling towards us, rearing its arched neck high up, so that the light of the sun shining through it made it transparent, and in the middle of the clear green mass we saw a long dark body suspended, borne along helplessly. It was a large shark that, venturing too near the beach, had been carried up by the breaker; he floated there a moment, erect on his tail, his fins beating impotently, when the roller broke and he was dashed with a loud thud on the beach; then the recoil of the surf swept him seawards and we saw no more of him.

===Chinese===
我们下到东侧的滩头，登岸处就在那里，正对着珊瑚礁石间的那道水道。这里的沙坡陡陡地插入颇深的水中；待天气许可之时，显然是把小艇抢滩上岸的绝佳地点。这天虽无风，大洋的涌浪却高，但见一道道碧绿的大浪庄严地卷将上来，直逼滩前，随即翻卷过去，迸碎成四散的晶莹水雾，好一派壮观景象。我们正立着赏看，忽见一桩奇景。一道浪朝我们涌来，高高昂起拱起的浪颈，阳光照透浪身，把它映得透明；就在那一团碧绿的正中，我们看见一条长长的黑影悬着，身不由己地随浪而行。那是一条大鲨鱼，冒险游得离滩太近，被卷岸的碎浪托了起来；它直立了一瞬，尾巴撑着，鱼鳍徒劳地拍打，随即浪头崩碎，它砰的一声大响被掼在滩上；回头浪又把它扫向海去，我们再没见到它的踪影。

===Original===
Having carried out the object of our journey, we filled our bottle with water and set forth on our return march. We recrossed Sugarloaf Col and tramped along the sands. There was no wind and the day was terribly hot. The sands reflected the burning sun into our faces, and we felt as if we were literally roasting. Now and then we lay down, clothes and all, in the saltwater pools, to cool ourselves, and we rolled handkerchiefs round our heads, which we kept constantly wet. As my hat had disappeared over a precipice on the previous day, this was a very necessary precaution against sunstroke, so far as I was concerned.

===Chinese===
此行的目的既已达成，我们灌满水瓶，踏上归程。重又翻过糖面包山隘，沿着沙滩跋涉。无风，天热得可怕。灼灼的日光从沙面反射到我们脸上，人简直像在被活活烤着。我们不时长衣长裤地躺进咸水洼里凉快一下，又把手帕裹在头上，不住地打湿。我的帽子前一天已经跌下绝壁不见了，所以单就我而言，这番防日射病的措施实在万不可省。

===Original===
When we were not far from our previous night's camp, we saw what appeared to be an easier way up the mountains than the one by which we had come down. The precipitous step at the top of the landslip had been difficult enough to descend, and on account of the rottenness of its substance we felt that the ascent might be impossible.

===Chinese===
走到离前夜的营地不远时，我们望见一条上山的路，看去比我们下来的那条好走些。滑坡顶上那道陡坎，下来时已够艰难；又因岩质朽烂，我们担心上行恐怕根本无望。

===Original===
Whether this new way of ours would have led us to the plateau of tree-ferns high above us, I cannot tell; but I doubt it. At any rate, we abandoned it before we had satisfied ourselves as to whether it was a practicable route or not, for a most excellent reason on Trinidad—the want of water. We had exhausted our bottle, and were clambering up difficult declivities on hands and knees, with the fierce sun blazing down upon our backs. As there was no wind, the air that lay on the roasting rocks was so oppressive that we had to rest frequently, and lie on our backs panting for breath.

===Chinese===
我们这条新路究竟通不通得到头顶高处的树蕨高台，我说不上来；不过我深表怀疑。无论如何，还没等我们弄清它是不是一条走得通的路，我们便弃了它，理由在特林达迪再充分不过——缺水。瓶里的水已耗尽，我们手脚并用地在难行的陡坡间攀爬，烈日当背直晒。四下无风，伏在滚烫岩石上的空气闷热难当，我们只得频频歇脚，仰面躺倒，喘息不已。

===Original===
I was in the worse condition of the two, in consequence of the loss of my hat, for, when the thin handkerchief I had wrapped round my head was dry, it was altogether insufficient for protection, and I ran some risk of being struck down by sunstroke or heat-apoplexy.

===Chinese===
两人之中我的情形更糟，皆因丢了帽子：裹在头上的那块薄手帕一旦干了，便全然不足以护头，我颇有几分被日射病或热中风击倒之虞。

===Original===
Accordingly, as we saw no signs of water above us, and as it was more than likely that this way would lead us to inaccessible precipices which would drive us back again, we thought it prudent to retrace our steps before we were quite exhausted, and make our way to the stream we knew of. We could rest by it until the sun had dipped below the mountaintops, and then resume our climb in the shade.

===Chinese===
于是，头顶既不见水的迹象，这条路又十有八九要引我们撞上攀不上去的绝壁、再逼我们折返，我们觉得还是趁力气未尽先退回来为妥，直奔我们认得的那道溪流去。可以在溪边歇到日头沉入山顶之下，再趁着荫凉继续攀登。

===Original===
We descended to the beach, and walked along the sands until we came to the rock under which we had camped on the previous night, and then, being opposite to our ravine, we struck out inland towards it across the down of beans. We must have turned rather to the right of the track we had followed on the previous day, for we suddenly came to a terrace of stones which we had not seen before, and which had evidently formed part of the Portuguese settlement. We clambered up this, and then perceived, still further to the right, the ruins of several huts and walls, built of unhewn stones and overgrown with the creeping beans. Most of the huts were built at the edge of a deep steep gully. As soon as we saw this, the same idea struck both of us: the Portuguese would most certainly have chosen the vicinity of a stream for their settlement, and in all probability there was running water at the bottom of that gully.

===Chinese===
我们下到滩边，沿沙滩走到前一晚宿营的那块岩石跟前；这里正对我们那道谷，便离开海岸，横穿豆子蔓生的草冈向谷里插去。我们准是比前一日循的路线偏右了些，因为忽然来到一道垒石阶地跟前——我们从未见过它，它显然是葡萄牙人屯居地的一部分。我们攀上阶地，又看见更靠右的地方有几座茅屋与墙垣的废墟，俱以毛石垒成，缠满了蔓生的豆藤。大多数茅屋都建在一道又深又陡的沟壑边上。一见这情形，我们俩不约而同起了同一个念头：葡萄牙人择屯居之地，必定挑在溪流近旁，这道沟底多半有活水。

===Original===
As it would not take us much out of our way to satisfy our curiosity, we climbed over the bean-covered rocks until we came to the edge of the gully, and, looking over, saw, to our delight and astonishment, not a tiny issue trickling drop by drop, like most of the streams of these ravines, but a regular little river of sparkling water, rushing down with a merry noise over the stones.

===Chinese===
满足这份好奇心并不须多绕多少路，于是我们攀过覆满豆藤的岩石，来到沟壑边缘，探头一望，又惊又喜——所见并非这些谷中常见的、一滴一滴渗出的涓细泉流，而是一条像模像样的小河，晶亮的水在石上欢畅地喧响着奔下坡去。

===Original===
We drank our fill, and found the water cool and delicious, but slightly fishy in flavour, for the large white gannets thronged the hills above. This is the most considerable stream on the island, and the only one that reaches the weather shore, all the others, as I have explained, being sucked up high above by the slopes of debris. This drains an extensive area, and several ravines meet at the head of the gully, each contributing its share of water. Among others was one of the ravines we had attempted to descend on the previous day, and which had led us to the brink of the precipice. From below we could now see the whole face of that precipice—a fearful wall of black rock, with a thin thread of water falling over it.

===Chinese===
我们喝了个痛快，只觉水又清凉又甘美，只是略带鱼腥——上头的群山上正糜集着那种白色大塘鹅。这是全岛水量最足的一条溪，也是唯一流得到向风岸的一条；如我先前解释过的，其余各条都在高处便被碎石坡吸干了。它汇集一大片地域的来水，好几道谷在沟头上相聚，各自捐出一份水。其中就有我们前一天试图下去、结果被引到绝壁边缘的那道谷。此刻从下面望去，那面绝壁的整幅壁身尽收眼底——一堵骇人的黑岩高墙，一线细流贴着墙身跌落。

===Original===
We walked down the gully, and found that the stream, not only crossed the down, but flowed right across the sands into the sea, the volume of water being too great to allow of its being all swallowed up by the thirsty soil on the way. We should have been more comfortable in our camp on the night before had we known there was a stream so near to us, and would have drunk our fill, instead of doling out to each other thimblefuls of water with a grudging hand. It was strange, too, that I had not discovered this river when I was here before. I had then, on descending from the mountains, turned to the right, even as we had done on the previous day, and suffered much from want of water; whereas, had I turned to the left, I should have come upon this generous supply after a few minutes' walk.

===Chinese===
我们顺沟而下，发现这条溪不但流过草冈，还径直穿过沙滩入海——水量太大，沿途干渴的泥土吞它不尽。前一晚扎营时若知道近旁便有这么一条溪，我们就舒服多了，尽可以开怀畅饮，而不必你推我让，吝啬地一顶针一顶针互相分水。奇怪的是，我上次在此地竟没有发现这条河。那回我从山上下到平地，也是朝右拐了——正和我们前一日一样——吃足了缺水的苦；其实只须朝左一拐，走上几分钟，便能遇到这股慷慨的水源。

===Original===
This was, indeed, a most valuable discovery for us, for now, should the supply of water fail in Southwest Bay, our working-party would merely have to cross the Sugarloaf Col, and follow the sands to this river—no very arduous journey.

===Chinese===
这对我们委实是个极有价值的发现：今后纵使西南湾断了水，我们的工作队也只须翻过糖面包山隘，沿沙滩走到这条河边——这段路算不得怎样艰辛。

===Original===
The heat had been so intense this day that our recent vain climb upon the mountainside had somewhat exhausted us, and we did not feel prepared to accomplish the whole of the long journey to the pier before dark; moreover, the position of the sun showed us that it was long past noon, and we should have had to hurry along without pause, in order to save our daylight.
