The doctor reported Arthur Cotton as being ill, and unfit for further digging for the present; so he was left on board with me, while George went on shore to take his place. The doctor promised to come off for me on the following morning, so that I could pay a short visit to the shore and inspect the works﻿—provided, of course, the surf permitted. Then we bade each other farewell, and the working-party returned to the bay.

===Chinese===
医生报告说阿瑟·科顿病了，眼下干不得挖掘的活计；于是他留在船上陪我，乔治则上岸顶了他的缺。医生答应第二天早晨来接我，好让我到岸上作一次短暂探访、察看工程——当然，还得碎浪许可。随后我们彼此道别，工作队返回湾里去了。

===Original===
The boat did not come off for me on the following day, as the surf was dangerous in Southwest Bay; and I held no communication with the shore-party for another week. During this time the wind was from the southeast; but though it rushed down the ravine with the usual violent squalls, it was moderate outside, and we had no more of the heavy sea which had been running throughout the previous week. It would have been possible for me to have landed at the pier on nearly any day, but there was still a sufficient surf to prevent our carrying off any more stones from the shore.

===Chinese===
第二天小艇没有来接我，因为西南湾里碎浪凶险；此后整整一个星期，我同岸上的队伍音讯不通。这段时间里，风一直从东南来；但尽管它照旧夹着惯常的猛烈阵风顺着裂谷冲刮下来，外海的风势却算温和，头一个星期连绵不断的大浪也没有再起。我几乎哪天都能在天然码头登岸，可碎浪仍不算小，我们再没法从岸上运石头回来了。

===Original===
We were anchored on a sandy bottom, but we could feel, by the grumbling of our chain as the yacht swung, that there were many rocks under us as well. These caused us a good deal of annoyance; for on several occasions, when the vessel was lying right over her anchor, the slack of the chain would take a turn round a rock and give us a short nip; so that when a swell passed under us, the vessel could not rise to it, but was held down by the tautened chain, which dragged her bows under, producing a great strain. The rocks must have been of brittle coral formation, for, after giving two or three violent jerks as the sea lifted her, the vessel would suddenly shake herself free with a wrench, evidently by the breaking away of the obstruction. At last all the projecting portions of the coral rock in our immediate neighbourhood must have been torn off, the chain having swept a clear space for itself all round, for after a time we were no longer caught in this way. These great strains loosened our starboard hawsepipe badly, so that we had to slip our chain and pass it through the other hawsepipe.

===Chinese===
我们锚泊在沙质海底上，但游艇来回回转时锚链那一阵阵闷响，让我们觉出脚下还藏着许多礁石。这些礁石教我们大伤脑筋；因为有好几回，船正正压在锚上方，链子的松弛段便绕上一块礁石，把我们紧紧咬住一下；于是一当涌浪从船底滚过，船身浮不起来，反被绷紧的锚链死死拽住，链子把船头拖进水里，吃力极大。这些礁石想必是脆性的珊瑚构造，因为海浪把船抬起、船身猛挫两三下之后，她会猛然一挣脱出身来，显见得是绊阻之物折断了。到后来，近旁珊瑚礁所有凸出的棱角想必全被磨掉，锚链替自己四下里扫出了一圈干净地盘，因为过了一阵，我们便再没这样被咬住过。这几番大力拉扯，把右舷的锚链筒弄得很有些松脱，我们只得把锚链解脱出来，改从另一舷的锚链筒穿过去。

===Original===
On December 9, it being a very fine day, I made an expedition in the dinghy toward the north end of the island. We found no good landing-place in that direction, for a coral ledge extends along the whole coast, causing broken water, and there are dangerous rocks in the midst of the breakers. We pulled into several little bays, each hemmed in by inaccessible barren mountains, so crowded with birds that, from the sea, the black crags looked quite white with them. We pulled inside Bird Island and inspected the Ninepin from close to. This huge cylinder of rock, 900 feet in height, is described by old navigators as having been crowned with large trees. It is now completely bare of vegetation, as it also was when I first saw it in 1881. I observed that, since my last visit, a huge mass had fallen off the top of it, which now lay by its side in shattered fragments. We caught a quantity of fish in these bays, one a fine fellow weighing thirty pounds; and we saw several large turtle floating on the water, but they sank as soon as we got near them.

===Chinese===
12 月 9 日，天色极好，我乘小划艇朝岛的北端作了一次远足。那个方向找不到好的登岸处，因为一道珊瑚礁台沿着整段海岸延展，搅得碎浪翻腾，浪花当中还立着凶险的礁石。我们划进好几座小湾，每座都被人迹难至的荒山环抱，鸟群密密匝匝，从海上望去，黑色的峭岩竟被它们映得一片白。我们划进鸟岛的内侧，近前察看了「九柱石」。这座高达九百英尺的巨石柱，据老一辈航海家记述，顶上昔日曾有大树成冠。如今它寸草不生，我 1881 年初见它时也是这般光秃。我看出自上次造访以来，它顶上又崩落了一大块，如今碎裂成块，卧在它旁边。我们在这些湾里钓到不少鱼，其中一条好汉足足三十磅重；还看见几只大海龟浮在水面上，可我们刚一靠近，它们就沉了下去。

===Original===
The uneventful days passed by, and I grew stout on laziness, salt beef, and duff. At last, on December 14, we pulled off in the dinghy to Southwest Bay, to see how the shore-party was getting on. We took with us a signal code book and the flags, so as to converse with our diggers in case we could not effect a landing﻿—a feat not to be attempted with our rotten little dinghy except under the most exceptional circumstances. The shore-party was, of course, also provided with a code book and set of flags.

===Chinese===
无事的日子一天天过去，我在懒惰、咸牛肉和布丁里养得发起福来。终于，12 月 14 日，我们乘小划艇划往西南湾，去看看岸上队伍的近况。我们随身带了信号码书和旗子，以便万一登不了岸，还能同我们的挖掘手们通话——坐着我们这条朽烂的小划艇登岸这桩壮举，除非情况万分特殊，是断不敢尝试的。岸上的队伍自然也备有一本码书和一套旗。

===Original===
As I required some more specimens of birds, I took with me, not a gun with which to shoot them, but simply a ramrod, the end of which I had loaded with a piece of lead. With this, as I sat in the boat, I found no difficulty in knocking down the inquisitive birds as they flew just over our heads, and I thus procured several good specimens.

===Chinese===
我还需要一些鸟的标本，随身带的不是打鸟的枪，而只是一根通条，一头坠了一块铅。坐在艇里，拿这家伙去敲那些恰巧从头顶掠过的多事的鸟，我发现毫不费力，就这样弄到了几件好标本。

===Original===
When we had pulled round the point and were in Southwest Bay we saw the white tents of the camp in front of us, and we could plainly distinguish, in a ravine behind, the great trench which the men had dug at the side of the cliff. We found little surf in the bay, but I would not risk a landing; for it would not require much bumping to knock our dinghy’s ancient bottom off; so we remained outside the breakers and signalled: “Any news?”

===Chinese===
绕过海岬进了西南湾，营地的白色帐篷便展现在眼前，背后的裂谷里，众人在峭壁旁掘出的那道大壕沟也认得分明。湾里碎浪不大，可我不肯冒险登岸；因为用不了几下磕碰，就足以把我们小划艇那副老船底给磕散了；于是我们留在碎浪线外，打出信号：「有什么新闻吗？」

===Original===
There was no reply with the flags, but some of the men walked down to the rocks under the Sugarloaf, so that we could come near enough to them to hail. A very disreputable lot our friends looked, too: as unkempt and rough as the original pirates might have been. The costume of each consisted merely of shirt, trousers, and belt, some sort of an apology for a hat crowning all. They were all more or less ragged, and were stained from head to foot with the soil in which they had been digging, so that they presented a uniform dirty, brownish yellow appearance, and, from a passing vessel, might easily have been taken for Brazilian convicts.

===Chinese===
对方没有用旗子回答，但几个人走到糖面包山下方的礁石上，好让我们凑得够近，喊得上话。我们这班朋友瞧着也实在不体面：蓬头垢面、粗野不羁，同当年那伙正牌海盗相比，只怕不遑多让。每人的行头不过衬衫、裤子加一条腰带，头顶上再勉强扣着个聊胜于无的帽子。个个身上都有些褴褛，从头到脚沾满他们挖掘的泥土，呈现出一律的污黄颜色，打旁边经过的船望过来，多半会把他们当成巴西的苦役犯。

===Original===
They shouted what news they had to tell. They reported that they were progressing well with the digging, and that they had caught a number of turtle. They promised to come off to the yacht the next morning, surf permitting. I made some sketches of Treasure Bay and West Bay as seen from the sea, and then returned to the vessel, to skin my birds.

===Chinese===
他们把要讲的新闻喊给我们听。据报告，挖掘进展顺利，还捉到了一批海龟。他们答应第二天早晨到游艇上来——只要碎浪许可。我画了几张从海上望见的宝藏湾和西湾的速写，然后回到船上，剥制我的鸟。

===Original===
The whaleboat was alongside on the following morning, December 15, and the doctor, Powell, Pollock, and two paid hands, boarded us. They had brought off some fresh and salted turtle and a quantity of turtle-eggs.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，12 月 15 日，捕鲸艇靠上了船舷，医生、鲍威尔、波洛克和两名受雇水手上了我们的船。他们带来了一些鲜海龟肉和腌海龟肉，还有一批海龟蛋。

===Original===
The yacht had now been lying off Trinidad for twenty-five days, and the shore-party had been hard at work for seventeen days; so I thought it was quite time for me to join the camp, and do my share of the work. I could see that the energetic doctor was anything but anxious to change the hard labour on shore for the lazy life on board ship, and though, as mate, he would have been the proper person to take charge of the vessel during my absence on land, still we considered it advisable to arrange matters differently.

===Chinese===
游艇在特林达迪海外已停泊了二十五天，岸上的队伍也已苦干了十七天；我想，该是我进驻营地、干上我那份活的时候了。我看得出来，那位精力充沛的医生一点儿也不想拿岸上的苦工，去换船上那份清闲日子；虽说以大副的身份，我上岸期间由他照管船只本是顺理成章，我们还是觉得另作安排更为妥当。

===Original===
The doctor, as I have said, was a most useful man on shore, and, as we were anxious to complete our operations as quickly as possible and leave the island before the stormy season should set in, it seemed a pity to waste so much energy and muscle as his in an idle life on board the yacht. Having remained at anchor for so long, and knowing that our anchor had now got such a firm hold that there was but little chance of its dragging, and having, moreover, discovered by experience that it was possible to ride where we were even in bad weather, I had acquired a considerable confidence in the safety of the vessel, and I believe that she could have remained off the cascade for six months without suffering damage. I, therefore, now came to the conclusion that it would not be very imprudent to leave a somewhat incompetent person in charge, as the chances were that he would have nothing to do. Pollock, who had complained of slackness for some time, was the one from whom the least amount of work could be extracted on shore, and was, therefore, the one who could be the most easily spared. I, consequently, decided to leave him on board the yacht, instead of the doctor.

===Chinese===
我说过，医生在岸上是极得力的一个人；我们既急于尽快完成工程、赶在风暴季节来临之前离岛，把他那样充沛的精力和筋骨，浪费在游艇上的闲散生活里，未免可惜。我们已锚泊了这么久，知道锚如今抓得极牢，走锚的可能微乎其微；而且经验又证明，纵使天气恶劣，泊在原地也撑得住；于是我对这艘船的安全已有了相当的信心，相信她在瀑布外海这么泊上六个月，也不致受损。因此，我这时得出结论：留一个稍稍不那么顶事的人看船，也算不得十分轻率，因为多半根本不会有什么事要他做。波洛克抱怨身子发乏已有些时日，在岸上能从他身上榨出的活计最少，因而也是最容易匀出来的一个。于是我便决定把他留在游艇上，而不用医生。

===Original===
The weather now looked very settled and there was little chance of bad weather for a time. I gave Pollock his instructions, and left with him, as a crew, Ted Milner and George Spanner. I packed up my traps and pulled off with the others to the bay, not at all sorry to do a little work, for a change.

===Chinese===
这时天气看去十分稳定，一时也不大可能变天。我给波洛克交代了事项，留特德·米尔纳和乔治·斯潘纳两名船员辅佐他。我收拾起自己的行装，同其余的人一道划往湾里，换个环境做点活计，心里倒一点也不觉得委屈。

===Original===
We took Jacko on shore with us. He did not admire the island, and particularly objected to the land-crabs. His favourite amusement was to turn on the tap of our tank, when no one was looking, and let all our hard-got supply of water run out.

===Chinese===
我们把杰科也带上了岸。它不喜欢这座岛，尤其讨厌那些陆蟹。它最得意的消遣，是趁没人瞧见时拧开我们水箱的龙头，让我们辛苦积攒的存水淌个精光。

===Original===
He behaved very well on the whole, however, except on Christmas Day, when he drank some rum which he found at the bottom of a pannikin, and, I am grieved to say, became disgracefully intoxicated. He had a dreadful headache the next day.

===Chinese===
不过总的说来，它表现得很乖巧，只有圣诞节那天是例外：它喝了在小铁杯底找到的一些朗姆酒，说来痛心，醉得不成体统。第二天，它头疼得厉害。

## XVII / 十七

===Original===
Pick and Shovel

===Chinese===
十字镐与铁锹

===Original===
As it was a Sunday there was no work done on the first day of my stay in camp; all hands had the usual holiday, which they chiefly employed in fishing, and in mending their clothes. I walked up the ravine and was surprised to find that so much of the landslip had been already removed. The trench was about twenty feet broad, and ultimately attained a depth of upwards of twenty feet in places. It extended for some distance along the face of the cliff﻿—if that term can be properly applied to a steep slope of a sort of natural concrete, a compact but somewhat brittle mass of stones and earth. It was at the foot of this cliff that we expected to find the cave described by the pirate, but how far we should have to dig down through the accumulation of earth and rocks that had fallen from above and now filled up the bottom of the ravine it was not easy even to conjecture.

===Chinese===
我进营的头一天恰逢星期日，照例不干活；全体人员放了例假，多半拿来钓鱼、缝补衣裳。我沿着裂谷走上去，惊见滑坡竟已被清除了这么多。壕沟约有二十英尺宽，最终有些地方深达二十英尺以上。它沿着峭壁的立面延展了相当一段——倘若「峭壁」一词当真能恰当地安在一道陡坡头上的话：那坡是一种天然的混凝土，是石块与泥土压成的致密而略嫌松脆的一体。我们指望在这峭壁脚下找到海盗描述过的洞穴，至于还得往下挖多深、穿过多少从上方坍落而今填满谷底的积土乱石，那可连臆测都不容易。

===Original===
Our object, it will be seen, was to clear the face of the cliff until we came to the original bottom of the ravine. Though the cliff was, as I have explained, composed of brittle matter, as if in an intermediate state between earth and rock, and of comparatively modern formation, it was easy to distinguish it from the much looser soil of the landslip that lay along its sides; this last, too, was of a very different colour, being reddish brown, whereas the cliff was slate-blue.

===Chinese===
可以看出，我们的目标是要清理峭壁的立面，一直清到裂谷原本的谷底。这峭壁虽如我解释过的那样，由松脆之物构成，仿佛处在土与石之间的过渡状态，生成的年代也较晚近，但要把它同贴在它两侧、松散得多的滑坡浮土区分开来，却并不费事；这浮土的颜色也截然不同，是红棕色，而峭壁则是板岩青。

===Original===
The men had constructed several little paths leading from the trench, down the ravine, to the edges of the chasms and precipitous steps which are frequent in this gully, and the earth and stones that were dug out of the trench were carried down these paths in the wheelbarrows and tilted over the precipices. As we gradually filled up these chasms the roads had to be extended further down the ravine, and at last we had formed a great dyke which stretched right across it. I was satisfied that all the operations had been conducted with judgment, and, if the treasure were in the ravine at all, there was but little doubt that we should find it.

===Chinese===
众人已经修出了几条小路，从壕沟顺着裂谷往下，通到这道沟谷里比比皆是的裂罅和陡坎边上；从沟里挖出的泥土石块，装进独轮车沿这些小路运下去，倒下悬崖。随着这些裂罅渐渐填满，道路只得向裂谷更下游延展，到最后我们竟筑成了一道横贯全谷的大堤。各项工程都办得有章有法，我很满意；倘若宝藏果真在这道裂谷里，我们找得到它，几乎没有多少可怀疑的余地。

===Original===
The same rules that had been laid down by the doctor for the discipline of the camp were observed during my stay on shore. All hands turned out at dawn, and cocoa and biscuit were served out. Then we worked hard from half-past five till nine, at which hour the temperature in that closed in ravine became so high that it was quite impossible even for a black man to work with pick and shovel. A bath in the sea, to refresh ourselves and wash off the clinging red dust, was our next proceeding. Then we put off our working clothes for others, and partook of a good breakfast, consisting chiefly of oatmeal, which we found by experience was the best food to work on. During the heat of the day we lay in our tents, almost panting for breath at times, so intolerably hot and close it was. At half-past three we returned to the ravine and did another three hours’ work. After this was another bath, then supper. There was a whole holiday on Sunday and a half holiday on Wednesday.

===Chinese===
我住在岸上期间，医生为营地纪律制定的那些规矩依然照行。天一亮全体起身，发放可可和硬面饼。随后从五点半苦干到九点；一到这个钟点，这道闭塞的裂谷里温度便高得叫人抡镐挥锹都干不下去，连黑人也不行。接下来便是下海洗个澡，振作精神，冲掉黏在身上的红尘。然后脱下工装换上别的衣裳，吃一顿丰盛的早餐，主要是燕麦粥——经验证明，干活最顶用的吃食就是它。白天最热的时辰，我们躺在帐篷里，有时几乎喘不上气来，那闷热实在教人受不了。三点半回到裂谷，再干三个钟头。这之后又洗一个澡，然后开晚饭。星期日全天休息，星期三歇半天。

===Original===
Even during the early hours of the morning, when the sides of the ravine shaded us from the sun, digging was hot and trying work for white men. We were, of course, bathed in perspiration all the while, and were, consequently very thirsty, so that the cook was kept busily employed in going backwards and forwards between camp and trench to refill our water-bottles.

===Chinese===
即便在一大早、裂谷两侧替我们遮着太阳的时辰，挖掘对白人也是又热又熬人的活计。我们自始至终汗出如浴，因而口渴得厉害，厨子只得在营地与壕沟之间来来回回地奔忙，替我们把水壶灌满。

===Original===
In the middle of the day the sun, blazing on the sands, made them terribly hot. No one could step on them with bare feet, even for a moment; one could not even lay one’s hand on the ground.

===Chinese===
正午时分，烈日炙烤着沙滩，把沙晒得烫得吓人。谁也不能赤脚在上面落脚，哪怕一下也不行；连把手按到地面上都按不住。

===Original===
The sand here is mixed with a finely granulated black mineral substance, and I think it is the presence of this that causes so great an absorption of heat. I have never found sands elsewhere, even in the Sahara, attain so high a temperature.

===Chinese===
这里的沙里混着一种细粒的黑色矿物，我想正是这东西作祟，沙才吸热吸得这样厉害。我从未见过别处的沙——连撒哈拉的也不例外——升到过这样高的温度。

===Original===
We were not altogether lazy out of digging hours. One’s clothes had to be washed, water had to be brought down in breakers and demijohns from the distant issue in the cliffs, and firewood had to be gathered. We sometimes went out in a body to perform this last duty. We would climb high up the mountainsides, where the dead trees lay thickest, and throw down the timber before us as we descended, until we had accumulated a large quantity at the bottom.

===Chinese===
不挖土的时辰，我们倒也并不全然偷懒。衣裳要洗，水要用淡水桶和细颈大坛从峭壁间那处远远的出水运下来，柴要拾。有时我们便倾队出动，去干最后一桩差事。我们爬到山坡高处枯树躺得最密的地方，一路往下走、一路把木料朝前扔下去，直到山脚下攒起一大堆。

===Original===
I shared one of the tents with Pursell, while the doctor and Powell occupied the other. On my first night on shore we caught three turtle. Our black cook, who was always looking out for them, came to my tent and reported that, while prowling about the beach, he had observed several large females crawling up the sands. It was a very dark night, so, taking a lantern, four of us set out. We soon came across one of the creatures, and followed her quietly until she had reached a spot far above high-water mark, and then we turned her over on her back. This is by no means an easy undertaking when one has to deal with a seven-hundred-pound turtle, and requires at least four men to carry it out. The turtle does not permit this liberty to be taken with her without offering considerable resistance: with her powerful flippers she drives the sand violently into the faces of her aggressors, attempting to blind them, so that caution has to be observed in approaching her. We turned over three turtle, and, on the following day, salted down the meat that we could not eat in a fresh state.

===Chinese===
我同珀塞尔合住一顶帐篷，医生和鲍威尔住另一顶。上岸后的头一夜，我们就捉了三只海龟。我们的黑人厨子一向替它们留神，来到我帐篷里报告说，他在海滩上转悠时，望见几只硕大的母龟正往沙上爬。那夜天黑得很，于是提上一盏灯，我们四个出发了。不久就撞见一只，便悄悄跟着它，直到它爬到远高出满潮线的地方，我们才把它翻了个肚皮朝天。对付一只七百磅重的海龟，这绝非轻而易举的事，至少得四个人才办得成。海龟绝不会任人这般摆布而不奋力抗拒：它用强有力的鳍肢把沙子狠狠扇到冒犯者的脸上，想把他们的眼睛迷住，因此接近它时须得留神。我们一共翻倒了三只，第二天便把鲜着吃不完的肉腌了起来。

===Original===
Turtle are kept alive for weeks on board ship, even in the tropics, and all the care that is taken of them consists in placing pillows under their heads, as they lie on their backs on deck﻿—so as to prevent apoplexy, I suppose﻿—and in throwing an occasional bucket of water over them. These creatures seem to be able to do without food for a very long period. We found that we could not employ this method of keeping alive the turtle we caught, for, though we constantly poured buckets of water over them, and shaded them with matting, they could not exist on these blazing sands; and the practice, cruel enough at sea, would have been much more so here.

===Chinese===
海龟在船上能一连活上几个星期，即便在热带也是如此；人们对它们的全部照料，不过是趁它们仰面躺在甲板上时，往它们头底下塞个枕头——我猜想是为了防止中风——再时不时浇上一桶水。这些生灵似乎能很长时间不吃东西。我们发觉没法用这个法子养活捉到的海龟，因为尽管我们不断往它们身上一桶桶地浇水，又用席子给它们遮阴，它们在这火烧似的沙滩上还是活不成；这法子在海上已够残忍，在这里就更残忍了。

===Original===
The paid hands enjoyed turtle-hunting, and were inclined, thoughtlessly, to turn over more turtle than were required for purposes of food; so that I had to give an order that no turtle should be turned over without leave, and the destruction of the creatures was strictly limited to the requirements of the larder. A similar law was made for the protection of the silly seabirds, and the only animals that could be slaughtered with impunity were the unfortunate land-crabs, for they had no friends among us to take their part and legislate on their behalf. They were now not nearly so plentiful in the vicinity of the camp as they had been. They had begun to give up their ignorant contempt for man, and on only one occasion during my stay on shore was it considered necessary for four of us to sally forth with sticks, before supper, and slay about a hundred each.

===Chinese===
受雇水手们喜欢猎龟，一时无心，动不动就把超出食用所需的龟也翻倒；于是我不得不下一道命令：未得许可，不得翻倒任何海龟，对这些生灵的宰杀，一律严格限制在伙房需要之内。为保护那些憨傻的海鸟，也立了类似的法规；唯一可以随意宰杀而不受罚的动物，只有倒霉的陆蟹，因为在我们中间没有谁肯替它们说话、替它们立法。这时营地左近的陆蟹已远不如先前那般众多。它们开始放弃那套对人的无知轻蔑；我住在岸上期间，只有一回，大家认为有必要在晚饭前提着棍子四人出击，每人击杀约一百只。

===Original===
The turtle were now so plentiful that we could have caught in a fortnight sufficient to last us for six months, had we even lived on nothing else. The Trinidad turtle are of large size﻿—500 to 700 pounds﻿—and their flavour is excellent. We had turtle-soup and turtle-steak every day for breakfast and dinner, so that we became utterly weary of the rich food, and I do not think any of us wish to see calipash or calipee for a long time to come.

===Chinese===
此刻海龟多得很，哪怕我们顿顿只吃它，两个星期里捉的也够撑上六个月。特林达迪的海龟个头硕大——五百到七百磅——滋味又极佳。我们天天早晚饭都是龟汤、龟排，这等肥腴的吃食吃得我们彻底倒了胃口，我想往后很长一段日子里，我们谁也不想再见到龟背肉（calipash）或龟腹肉（calipee）了。

===Original===
We did not neglect the other useful products of the island. We gathered the wild beans, and found them a very welcome addition to our diet. Of fish we always had plenty. Powell was our great fisherman, and was the inventor of the seine constructed of wire-fencing which I have already described. In addition to the edible fish I have mentioned as swarming in these waters there are several other species that we looked upon with some doubt, and refrained from eating. Some of these were of quaint forms and dazzling colours, so that their appearance seemed to warn us of their poisonous nature. There were fish of brilliant blue, others with stripes of white and purple, others with vermilion fins and yellow bands like those of a wasp. Sea-snakes abounded in the pools. These, according to an Italian cook we had on the *Falcon*, are edible; but we did not venture to try them. They attain the length of five feet and are of a grey colour, with yellow stripes. They appear to be of savage disposition, for, when harpooned, they twist about and bite with fury anything within their reach.

===Chinese===
岛上其他有用的出产，我们也没有怠慢。我们采来野豆子，发觉它们很受欢迎，给伙食添了新花样。鱼则从来管够。鲍威尔是我们这里的钓鱼大家，我已描述过的那张铁丝围网做的拉网，发明人便是他。除了我提过在这片水域里成群洄游的可食鱼类之外，还有几种我们看着犯嘀咕、没敢吃的鱼。有的模样古怪、颜色晃眼，那副长相就像在警告我们它们有毒。有通体亮蓝的鱼，有带白紫条纹的，有长着朱红鳍、黄条纹黄得像胡蜂腰带的。水潭里海蛇成群。据我们在猎鹰号上时的一位意大利厨子说，这东西能吃；但我们没敢尝试。它们能长到五英尺长，灰色，带黄条纹。看那性情颇为凶悍，因为一旦被鱼叉叉中，它们便狂扭身子，够得着什么就狂咬什么。

===Original===
I stayed on shore altogether for a fortnight, and kept a journal of our proceedings, which, together with several sketches, specimens of the flora, and other articles, were washed out of the lifeboat and lost when we abandoned the island. The loss of the journal, however, matters little, for our life on shore was almost devoid of incident, and was chiefly made up of monotonous work with pick, shovel, and wheelbarrow.

===Chinese===
我在岸上前后待了两个星期，把我们的大小事务记了一本日志；这本日志，连同几幅速写、几件植物标本和其他物件，后来在我们弃岛离去的当口，被海水从救生艇里冲了出去，就此丢失。不过日志的损失无甚要紧，因为我们岸上的生活几乎没有波澜，主要就是十字镐、铁锹加独轮车的单调活计。

===Original===
We dug away, still through loose soil that had evidently formed part of the landslip, and removed some thousands of tons; but we did not come to the foot of the cliff, or the cave which is described to be there. Some of the stones that we had to remove in the course of our digging were very large. We had a quantity of strong ropes and blocks on shore, and when we came across an exceptionally big rock, we clapped a number of watch-tackles one on the other, and, by putting all hands on the fall of the last tackle, we obtained a very powerful purchase, equivalent, I calculated on one occasion, to the power of five hundred men. We found bones and bits of decayed wood among the earth, but the former always proved to be the remains of a goat and not of a pirate, and the latter were the fragments of dead trees and not of chests of loot.

===Chinese===
我们一路挖下去，穿的仍是明显属于滑坡体的松土，清走了几千吨；却还没有挖到峭壁脚下，也没有挖到据说在那里的洞穴。挖掘途中必须清走的有些石块个头很大。我们在岸上备有不少粗绳和滑轮，碰上特别巨大的岩石，就把几组滑车一组搭一组地串起来，让全体人手齐拉最末一组滑车的力端绳，由此获得一股极其强劲的机械之力——有一回我估算过，抵得上五百个人的力气。我们在土里挖到过骨头和朽木碎片，但前者查来查去总是山羊的遗骸，不是海盗的；后者总是枯树的残片，不是赃物箱子的。

===Original===
But shortly before Christmas there were some encouraging signs. We had now got down to a considerable depth, and we noticed that, when a pick was driven into the bottom of the trench, a hollow sound was given out, as if we were on the roof of a cavern, and, occasionally, little holes would open out and the earth would slip down into some chasm underneath. We dug still deeper, and we came to a collection of very large rocks, which we were unable to move. They were jammed together, and evidently formed the roof of a cavern, for, wherever we could clear away the earth that lay between any two of these rocks, we looked down through the opening into a black, empty space, the bottom of which we could not touch by thrusting through our longest crowbar. This looked promising, for it was just such a cavern as this that we were seeking.

===Chinese===
但圣诞节前不久，出现了一些令人鼓舞的迹象。我们这时已下掘到相当的深度，发觉十字镐凿到沟底时会发出空洞的声响，仿佛我们正踩在一座洞穴的顶上；偶尔还会裂开一些小孔，泥土便溜进底下某道裂罅里去。我们挖得更深，碰到一堆硕大无朋的岩石，怎么也挪不动。它们彼此挤死在一起，显然构成了一座洞穴的顶盖，因为凡是能清走两块岩石之间浮土的地方，我们都能透过豁口俯瞰一片漆黑的空洞，把最长的那根撬棍捅下去也够不着底。这情形大有希望，因为我们苦苦寻找的，正是这样一座洞穴。

===Original===
We found that the rocks were too close together to allow of our effecting an entrance from above, so we dug down along the side of the last and largest of these until we came to its foot; and there indeed was a sort of cavern, partly filled up with loose earth, which we cleared out.

===Chinese===
我们发现这些岩石挤得太紧，没法从顶上打开入口，于是沿着最后也最大的一块的侧边往下挖，一直挖到它的脚下；那里果然有一座洞穴模样的所在，半填着松土，我们把土清了出来。

===Original===
There was no treasure in it, and nothing to show that any human being, before us, had ever visited the spot. I think it was at this stage of our operations that each man began very seriously to doubt whether we were searching in the right place at all, and whether there might not be some further clue that was missing, and, without which, search would almost certainly be futile. But, whatever may have been thought, there was, so far as I can remember, no expression given to these doubts, and each worked on with the same cheery will as at the beginning, even as if he were confident of success. These men were determined, in an almost literal sense, to leave no stone unturned, and not to abandon that ravine until they had satisfied themselves as to whether the treasure was or was not there.

===Chinese===
洞里没有宝藏，也没有半点迹象表明在我们之前曾有任何人到过此地。我想正是在工程的这个阶段，每个人心里都开始很认真地犯嘀咕：我们究竟是不是找对了地方；会不会还缺一条更进一步的线索，少了它，搜寻几乎注定徒劳。但无论心里怎么想，据我记忆所及，这些疑虑谁也没有说出口，人人照旧带着起初那股欢实的劲头干下去，俨然对成功深信不疑。这班人拿定了主意，几乎是照着字面的意思，非要「把每块石头都翻遍」，不弄清宝藏究竟在不在那里，绝不弃了那道裂谷。

===Original===
On the Sunday after my arrival on shore, December 22, we went off in the whaleboat to see how Pollock was getting on. The weather had been exceedingly fine throughout the week in Southwest Bay, and we might have launched the boat on almost any day; but, though there had been no heavy wind in the neighbourhood of the island, there had been a considerable swell at the anchorage for part of the time, and Pollock reported that the yacht had tumbled about a good deal. He had found opportunities for landing at the pier with the dinghy, and had brought off some breakers of water from the cascade and a quantity of firewood. He had been very lucky with his fishing, having caught several germanic, weighing from twenty to forty pounds apiece, and an abundance of other fish. Ted Milner was now taken on shore with us, while Arthur Cotton was left on board.

===Chinese===
我上岸后的第一个星期日，12 月 22 日，我们乘捕鲸艇出海，去看波洛克过得怎样。整整一个星期，西南湾的天气都极好，几乎哪天都可以放艇；但是，虽说岛近旁没有刮过大风，锚地里有一阵子却涌浪不小，波洛克报告说，游艇颠簸得不轻。他寻着机会乘小划艇在天然码头登了岸，从瀑布运回几桶淡水，还弄回不少柴火。他钓鱼的手气极好，钓到几条「热拉尼克」（germanic），每条重二十到四十磅，此外还有大量的其他鱼。这时特德·米尔纳跟我们上了岸，阿瑟·科顿则留在船上。

===Original===
We worked away steadily in the ravine until Christmas Day, when there was, of course, a holiday. We had a most luxurious dinner on shore, as also had the three men on board the vessel. The menu of our shore-dinner was as follows:﻿—Turtle soup, boiled hind-fish, curried turtle-steak, boiled salt junk, tinned plum-pudding. For vegetables we had preserved potatoes and carrots, and Trinidad beans. Good old rum was the only beverage. There were some other luxuries, chief of which was a box of cigars, which had been put away for this occasion. Christmas Day was intensely hot, so that we remained in our tents, having no energy for exploring mountains. With the exception of Jacko’s disgraceful intoxication, no incidents of note occurred.

===Chinese===
我们在裂谷里稳稳当当地干到圣诞节，那天自然放假。我们在岸上吃了一顿极尽奢华的正餐，船上那三个人也同样沾了光。我们这顿岸上大餐的菜单如下：——龟汤，清煮石斑鱼（hind），咖喱龟排，煮咸牛肉，罐装葡萄干布丁。蔬菜有罐头土豆、胡萝卜，还有特林达迪的豆子。上好的陈年朗姆酒是唯一的饮品。此外另有几样奢侈品，头一样是一盒雪茄，专为这个日子收藏至今。圣诞节那天热得厉害，我们便待在帐篷里，没有精力去探山。除了杰科那次不成体统的酗酒之外，没有发生什么值得一提的事。

===Original===
On the Sunday after Christmas Day, Pursell and myself set out to explore the weather side of the island, taking our lunch in our pockets﻿—biscuits, figs, rum, and tobacco. We crossed the Sugarloaf Col and descended upon the coast of Southeast Bay, then we turned to the right and followed the shore to the extreme south end of the island, where Noah’s Ark falls a sheer wall into the surf.

===Chinese===
圣诞节后的那个星期日，我和珀塞尔出发去探察岛的向风一面，午饭揣在口袋里——硬面饼、无花果、朗姆酒和烟草。我们翻过糖面包山隘，下到东南湾的海岸，然后向右转，沿着岸边一直走到岛的最南端，在那里，「诺亚方舟」如一堵笔直的高墙，直插进碎浪之中。

===Original===
There was a quantity of wreckage in this bay, and in one place we found a topmast and some ribs of a vessel which might have been the remains of the hull I had seen here nine years before. The broken bits of planks, timbers, barrels, hen-coops and other relics of ships, were piled quite thickly on the rocks above high-water mark, and we came across a square-faced gin bottle, full of fresh water, which, from its position, could not have been washed ashore, but must have been left here by some human being. We saw the footprints of turtle, showing that every sandy beach on this island is frequented by numbers of these creatures. In view of the threatened turtle-famine we read of, it might be worth someone’s while to come here for a cargo of them; but the difficulty of getting any quantity off alive would be great.

===Chinese===
这座湾里散落着大量船骸，有一处我们还发现一根中桅和若干船肋，说不定就是我九年前在此见过的那具船壳的残余。断裂的船板、木料、桶子、鸡笼和别的船上遗物，厚厚地堆在满潮线以上的礁石上；我们还撞见一只方棱瓶身的金酒瓶，盛着满满一瓶淡水，从它所在的位置看，绝计不是被浪冲上岸的，必定是哪个世人遗落在那里的。我们看见了海龟的足迹，可见这座岛上每一片沙滩，都有成群的这类生灵频频光顾。鉴于我们读到过的那种海龟行将绝迹之忧，专程来此装一船回去倒是值得；只是想把相当数量活着运走，难处可就大了。

===Original===
The scenery of East Bay is very extraordinary, for here the signs of volcanic action are more evident than on any other portion of the island. At the south end of the bay there is no sandy beach; masses of shattered rocks, fallen from above, strew the shore, and between these are solidified streams of black lava, which appear to have followed each other in successive waves, one having cooled before the next has poured down upon it, so that a series of rounded steps is formed. The ledges of lava extend far out to sea, producing a dangerous reef, on which the sea always breaks heavily.

===Chinese===
东湾的景色极为奇特，因为岛上再没有哪一处，火山活动的痕迹比这里更彰明较著。湾的南端没有沙滩；从上方崩落的碎岩大块大块地撒满岸边，碎岩之间是一条条凝固的黑色熔岩流，看去像一波接一波先后袭来，前一波冷却之后，后一波才浇覆其上，于是一道道圆浑的阶台就此层叠而成。熔岩台地远远伸入海中，形成一道凶险的礁石，海浪撞在上面，永远是雷霆万钧。

===Original===
As we advanced over the boulders there towered above us on our right hand the perpendicular side of Noah’s Ark, of a strange red colour, looking like molten iron where the sun’s rays fell upon it. A quantity of red debris from the roof of this mountain was also lying on the shore, and at the north end of it we observed that a gigantic couloir﻿—as it would be called in the Alps﻿—of volcanic ashes and lava sloped down from its summit to the gap which connects it with the Sugarloaf. It was obvious, from the vast amount of these fire-consumed debris and waves of lava surrounding its base, that Noah’s Ark had once been a very active volcano, and I think it highly probable that there is a crater at the top of it. Though it is perpendicular on three sides, it might be possible to ascend it from the fourth side, by the couloir connecting it with the gap under the Sugarloaf; but the attempt would be risky, and a slip on its steep, sloping roof would mean a drop over a wall 800 feet in height.

===Chinese===
我们在巨石间前行，右手边「诺亚方舟」笔直的侧壁在头顶巍然高耸，泛着一种奇异的红色，阳光落到哪里，哪里便像熔化的铁水。这座山「屋顶」上剥落的红色碎屑也大量躺在岸上；在它的北端，我们望见一道巨大的沟槽（couloir）——若在阿尔卑斯山，人们便这样称呼它——由火山灰和熔岩构成，从山顶斜落下来，直通它与糖面包山相连的那处山隘。山脚四周环绕着这么多火焚的碎屑和一道道熔岩的波浪，足见「诺亚方舟」当年是一座非常活跃的火山，我认为它顶上极有可能存在一个火山口。它三面皆是绝壁，从第四面、循着连接它与糖面包山下山隘的那道沟槽，兴许登得上去；但这一尝试颇为冒险，在它那陡斜的「屋顶」上失足一滑，便意味着从一面八百英尺高的墙上跌落下去。

===Original===
We clambered over the rocks until we came to the end of Noah’s Ark, and we stood on a ledge of lava and gazed at one of the strangest sights of this strange island. The base of the great red mountain is pierced by a magnificent tunnel, known as the Archway, which connects Southwest Bay with East Bay. What seem to be gigantic stalactites depend from its roof; and the different gradations of colour and shade on its rugged sides﻿—from glowing red in the blaze of the sun to terra-cotta, delicate pink, and rich purple, and then to deepest black in the inmost recesses﻿—produce a very beautiful effect. The heaving water is black within it, save where the white spray flashes; but, looking through it, one perceives, beyond, the bright green waves of Southwest Bay, and the blue sky above them.

===Chinese===
我们攀过乱石，来到「诺亚方舟」的尽头，站上一道熔岩台地，凝望这座奇岛最奇特的景致之一。大红山的山脚被一条壮丽的隧道洞穿，人称「拱道」（Archway），它把西南湾与东湾连通起来。洞顶垂着俨然巨型石钟乳的东西；它嶙峋的洞壁上，色彩与明暗层层递变——烈日灼照下的炽红，到赤陶色、淡粉、浓紫，再到最深处内窾的漆黑——生出极美的效果。洞里涌动的海水一片黑沉沉，唯有白色浪花闪过的地方是例外；但透过洞子望去，可以望见那一头西南湾亮绿的波涛，和波涛之上的蓝天。

===Original===
The sea does not flow freely through the tunnel, except at high-water; for, on the side we were standing, its mouth is crossed by a ledge of lava, which is left dry by the receding tide. But inside the tunnel there is deep water, and the ocean swell always penetrates it from Southwest Bay, dashing up its sides with a great roar, which is repeated in hoarse echoes by the mountain.

===Chinese===
除非在满潮时分，海水并不能畅快地流过隧道；因为在我们站的这一侧，洞口横着一道熔岩台地，退潮之后便露出干面。但隧道里面水很深，大洋的涌浪总是从西南湾灌进来，轰然撞上两侧洞壁，山又用沙哑的回声一遍遍应和。

===Original===
According to an ancient description of Trinidad quoted in the *South Atlantic Directory*, the Archway is 40 feet in breadth, 50 in height, and 420 in length. I think it far higher and broader than this﻿—at any rate, at its mouth. No doubt the action of the surf is continually enlarging it.

===Chinese===
据《南大西洋航海指南》所引的一段关于特林达迪的古老记述，「拱道」宽 40 英尺，高 50 英尺，长 420 英尺。我却觉得它远比这更高更宽——至少洞口是如此。毫无疑问，碎浪的侵蚀正在不断地把它扩大。

===Original===
Pursell and myself, having admired this beautiful scene for some time, turned back, crossed the rocky promontory of East Point, and proceeded along the sands till we came to the Portuguese settlement, which I wished to examine more carefully than I had been able to do when here with the doctor a month before.

===Chinese===
我和珀塞尔把这幅美景饱览了一阵，便转身折回，越过东角的石质海岬，沿着沙滩前行，一直走到葡萄牙人的屯居地——一个月前我同医生来此，未能细看，这一趟正要补上，把它察看得更仔细些。

===Original===
We had lunch by the side of the river which flows under the Portuguese ruins, and then commenced to explore. The Portuguese had certainly selected the only spot on the island at all suitable for a permanent settlement; for not only is there here the best supply of water, but there is also a considerable area of fairly fertile land, though it is greatly encumbered with rocks. The downs by the river are densely covered with beans, which also grow all over the ruined huts. It is possible that these beans were originally planted here by the settlers, and have since spread over all the downs between this and Southwest Bay; for they are not to be found on the other side of the island.

===Chinese===
我们在流经葡萄牙废墟下方的那条河边用了午饭，随后着手踏勘。葡萄牙人选中的，确是全岛唯一算得上适合长久定居的所在；因为此地不但有全岛最好的水源，还有一片颇为肥沃的可观地面，尽管乱石壅塞得厉害。河边的草冈上密密覆着豆子，坍毁的茅屋上也长得到处都是。这些豆子或许本是屯居者当初种下的，此后便蔓延开来，长满了从这里直到西南湾之间的所有草冈；因为岛的另一面并无它们的踪影。

===Original===
The huts, of which the rough walls of unhewn stone alone remain, are built in terraces one above the other on the hillside. A great deal of labour was evidently expended in the construction of these terraces, and of the roads leading to them, and quantities of stones had been piled-up in order to obtain a level surface. This must have been a picturesque little village in its day﻿—whenever that day was, for, though I have searched diligently, I can find no record to show at what period Trinidad was used as a penal settlement by the Portuguese. Amaso Delano, writing of his visit to the island in 1803, speaks of a “beach above which the Portuguese once had a settlement;” and a still older narrative alludes to a Portuguese penal establishment here as a thing of the long past. Malley, who was here in 1700, took Trinidad in the name of the King of England﻿—as I have already mentioned﻿—and he says nothing of such a settlement.

===Chinese===
那些茅屋如今只剩毛石垒成的粗墙，一层层台地相叠，筑在山坡上。修筑这些台地和通往台地的道路，显然耗费了大量劳力，还堆垒起大量的石头，好造出平整的基面。在它的全盛之日——且不论那是哪年哪月——这里想必曾是一座景致如画的小村；因为虽经我悉心搜求，仍找不到任何记载，能说明葡萄牙人在哪个时期把特林达迪用作流放犯的屯居地。阿马索·德拉诺写他 1803 年游历此岛的经过，提到「一处海滩，葡萄牙人曾在它的上方设过屯居地」；还有一部更古老的纪事，提到葡萄牙人在此的流放机构时，已把它说成遥远的往事了。哈雷于 1700 年至此，以英王之名占取了特林达迪——这一点我已提过——他对这样一处屯居地却只字未提。

===Original===
Near the huts we found places where the soil had been cleared of stones, for purposes of cultivation, and there were several walled-in enclosures.

===Chinese===
茅屋近旁，我们见到几处为耕种而拣净了石头的地面，还有几圈垒墙围起来的园场。

===Original===
We saw a good deal of broken pottery and tiles lying about, not such as we had discovered in Southwest Bay, of Oriental manufacture, but of a very rough description, probably homemade. For, on the top of a hill overlooking our ravine, we came across a hole that had evidently been dug for the purpose of extracting a sort of clay that is there, and there were signs of fire near it, and many fragments of earthenware, so we conjectured that we were looking at all that remained of the ancient Trinidad pottery-works.

===Chinese===
四下里散见不少破陶碎瓦，可不是我们在西南湾发现过的那一种东方制品，而是做工极粗的一路，多半是自制土货。因为在俯瞰我们那道裂谷的一座山顶上，我们碰到一个坑，显见得是为采挖那里的一种黏土而掘的，坑旁有生火的痕迹，还有许多陶器碎片，于是我们猜想，眼前这些便是古时特林达迪陶窑的全部遗存了。

===Original===
We did not return to Southwest Bay by the Sugarloaf Col, but by another route, which the shore-party had discovered in the course of a previous Sunday’s tour of exploration. This lay over the gap in the downs at the back of our bay, and presented no difficulties; but the soft soil and tangled vegetation made the climb a rather laborious one.

===Chinese===
回西南湾时我们没有走糖面包山隘，走的是另一条路——那是岸上队伍先前某个星期日出游探察时发现的。这条路从我们那处海湾背后的草冈间的垭口上翻过去，并无难处；只是土松草乱，爬起来颇为吃力。

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===
A Voyage to Market

===Chinese===
赶集的航程

===Original===
I remained on shore for a fortnight, during which the weather was fine, though a slight shower generally fell in the morning.

===Chinese===
我在岸上盘桓了两个星期，其间天气一直晴好，只是上午多半要落一阵小雨。

===Original===
We had still a large supply of stores, both on shore and on board; but there was one article of food which we were consuming in much larger quantities than had been anticipated﻿—the necessary oatmeal﻿—and it was now found that but very little of it was left. It was, therefore, decided that I should sail to Bahia﻿—our nearest market-town﻿—with the yacht, and procure some more.

===Chinese===
岸上船上，我们的给养都还充足；唯独有一宗吃食，消耗的分量远比预想的多——必不可少的燕麦片——如今一查，所剩已经无几。于是大家议定，由我驾着游艇驶往巴伊亚——离我们最近的集镇——再采办一批来。

===Original===
A voyage of 1,400 miles in order to purchase a little oatmeal sounds like a rather large order; but, as a matter of fact, it was more comfortable to be underway than to lie at anchor where we were, exposed to the ocean swell. So we did not look upon the journey as a troublesome duty.

===Chinese===
为买一点燕麦片而航行 1,400 英里，听上去像是一份颇为不小的「订单」；可事实上，航行在海上，比泊在原处任大洋涌浪摇晃舒服得多。所以我们并不把这趟旅程看作什么苦差。

===Original===
My crew was to consist of Pollock and the three white sailors.

===Chinese===
随我出海的船员，是波洛克和三名白人水手。

===Original===
I put Ted Milner, the boatswain, on Pollock’s watch, and took Arthur Cotton on mine. John Wright did the cooking and kept no watch, though he was always ready to lend a hand if necessary.

===Chinese===
我把水手长特德·米尔纳编进波洛克那一更，把阿瑟·科顿拨到我这一更。约翰·赖特管做饭，不上更，不过一旦需要，他随时乐意搭把手。

===Original===
On Sunday, December 29th, the whaleboat went off to the yacht for another load of stores, so that there might be an ample supply on the island during the absence of the vessel; for it was not possible to foresee how long we should be away.

===Chinese===
12 月 29 日，星期日，捕鲸艇又驶到游艇上装了一趟给养，好让船离岛期间岛上供应充裕；因为这一去要多久，谁也料不定。

===Original===
On Monday, 30th, I returned on board, and, after the two parties had bade each other goodbye and good luck, the whaleboat went off to the shore with a last cargo of provisions. We now got the vessel ready for sea. We unbent the storm-trysail and storm-foresail, and bent the large foresail; being rather short-handed, we left our topmast housed during this voyage.

===Chinese===
星期一，30 日，我回到船上；两拨人彼此道了别、互祝好运，捕鲸艇载着最后一船口粮驶向岸去。随后我们做出海准备：解下风暴斜桁帆和风暴前帆，换上大前帆；由于人手偏紧，这一航程我们索性让中桅收在桅座里不放。

===Original===
We did not weigh the anchor until 5 p.m.; we set the whole mainsail, the mizzen, foresail, and second jib. The wind, at first, was exceedingly light, so that we drifted helplessly about for a time, and we did not get clear of the island until after dark. I was thus unable to sail round to the mouth of Southwest Bay and satisfy myself that the boat had been safely beached. However, seeing that so many successful landings had been accomplished, I considered it unnecessary to hang about the island until the following daylight, so we shaped our course for Bahia. A moderate wind sprang up in the night and we soon left the island far behind us.

===Chinese===
我们直到下午 5 点才起锚，张起整幅主帆、后帆、前帆和二号艏帆。起初风极微弱，我们一时毫无办法地随波漂荡，直到天黑之后才算驶离了岛。这样一来，我便无法绕到西南湾湾口，亲眼确认小艇已平安抢滩。不过，想到先前已有那么多次成功的登岸，我看犯不着在岛边逗留到次日天光，于是把航向对准了巴伊亚。夜里起了和风，我们很快把这座岛远远抛在了身后。

===Original===
This was a most successful voyage. The wind was from the northeast all the time, right abeam, and therefore as favourable as it could be. There was not quite enough of it, however, and our best day’s work was only 154 miles. On one day it was rather squally, and we had to trice up the main tack now and then. The voyage only occupied five days, for we sighted the white sands and the coconut groves of the Brazilian coast at 5 p.m. on January 4, and at 7:30 we rounded St. Antonio Point, and entered the bay of Bahia. Here we found that a strong tide was running against us, and, as is usually the case in the gulf at this hour, there was scarcely any wind; so we were compelled to let go our anchor near the lighthouse. A Newfoundland barque that had followed us in had to do likewise.

===Chinese===
这是一趟极其顺遂的航行。风从头到尾都是东北风，正正吹在横舷上，因此顺得无以复加。只是风力略欠，我们成绩最好的一天也只走了 154 英里。有一天风阵性颇强，我们时不时得把主帆的受风下角吊起。全程统共只用了五天：1 月 4 日下午 5 点，我们望见了巴西海岸的白沙和椰林；7 点 30 分绕过圣安东尼奥角，驶入巴伊亚湾。到了这里，我们发觉一道强劲的潮流顶着我们流，而这个钟点湾里照例几乎没有风；我们只得在灯塔近旁抛锚。一艘跟着我们进湾的纽芬兰三桅帆船，也只得照样下了锚。

===Original===
The next day, January 5, we rose early and saw before us again the beautiful white city which we had left nearly two months before. We got up the anchor as soon as the morning breeze had sprung up, and sailed slowly to our anchorage under Fort la Mar, where we let go in three fathoms of water.

===Chinese===
第二天，1 月 5 日，我们一早起身，将近两个月前辞别的那座美丽的白色之城，又展现在眼前。晨风一起，我们便起锚，缓缓驶往拉马尔堡（Fort la Mar）下方的锚位，在三英寻深的水里抛了锚。

===Original===
We noticed that a strange flag was flying on all the forts and government buildings, as well as on the guard-ship and a little gunboat that was lying near us. It bore no resemblance to the flag of Brazil, or to that of any other nationality, and puzzled us somewhat.

===Chinese===
我们注意到，各处炮台和官署建筑上，连同守备舰和泊在我们近旁的一艘小炮艇上，都飘着一面陌生的旗。它既不像巴西的国旗，也不像任何别国的旗，教我们颇有些纳闷。

===Original===
Though it was Sunday, our old friend, the harbour doctor, came off to us in his launch. I was uncertain as to how he would receive us; for the regulations of Brazilian ports are strict, and our entry here was most informal. We had sailed out of Bahia, as the doctor himself must have known, two months before, presumedly for Sydney, Australia; and now, here we were again at Bahia, with no bill of health, and only half of our crew on board.

===Chinese===
这天虽是星期日，我们的老朋友港医仍旧乘着他的小汽艇到船上来。他会怎样接待我们，我心里没底；因为巴西各港的章程森严，而我们这次进港，实在是最不合规矩的。两个月前我们驶出巴伊亚——港医本人心里必然有数——名义上是要去澳大利亚的悉尼；而如今我们又回到了巴伊亚，健康证书没有一张，船上只剩半班人马。

===Original===
He came alongside, and we greeted each other. “What port do you come from?” he then asked.

===Chinese===
他靠上船来，我们互相问了好。「你们从哪个港口来？」他接着问。

===Original===
“We have been in no port since we left here,” I replied.

===Chinese===
「离开此地之后，我们没有进过任何港口。」我答道。

===Original===
“How﻿—in no port!” he exclaimed, raising his eyebrows in slight astonishment. He was too thorough a Brazilian to express much surprise at anything, or to rouse himself from the almost Oriental apathy of manner that distinguishes this somewhat indolent race.

===Chinese===
「怎么——没进过港口！」他扬了扬眉毛，略略露出讶异。但他是个地道的巴西人，无论何事都不至于大惊小怪，也不肯让自己从那副近乎东方人的倦怠神态中打起精神来——这份倦怠，正是这个略嫌懒散的民族的印记。

===Original===
Then I explained to him that we had been passing our Christmas holidays on the desert island of Trinidad, that I had left most of my companions there while I had sailed to Bahia for more stores, and that, having been in no inhabited port, I had, consequently, been unable to provide myself with a bill of health.

===Chinese===
于是我向他说明：我们的圣诞节是在荒岛特林达迪过的；我把大部分同伴留在岛上，自己驾船来巴伊亚添办给养；既然没进过任何有人烟的港口，自然也就无从置办健康证书。

===Original===
“And what were you doing on Trinidad all this time?”

===Chinese===
「这些日子，你们在特林达迪都做了些什么？」

===Original===
“Among other things, we were making collections of the fauna and flora. There are some rare birds.”

===Chinese===
「做了些事情，其中一样是采集动植物标本。那里有几种珍稀的鸟。」

===Original===
“Have you any specimens of the birds on board?”

===Chinese===
「船上有鸟的标本吗？」

===Original===
Luckily I had a few, and exhibited them. He was somewhat of a naturalist himself, and recognised one species which he had seen on Fernando Noronha.

===Chinese===
幸好我手头有几件，便取出来给他看。他自己也算得半个博物学家，认出了其中一个种，是他在费尔南多·迪诺罗尼亚见过的。

===Original===
He seemed satisfied, and gave us pratique without any demur.

===Chinese===
他看去是满意了，二话不说，便准了我们检疫放行（pratique）。

===Original===
Mr. Wilson had, of course, seen us, and had sent his boat to fetch me on shore. Leaving the others on board, I got into the boat, and, as the black boatman pulled me under the fort, it occurred to me to ask him, in the best Portuguese I could muster, what was the signification of the new flag that floated above the battlements. In my anxiety concerning pratique I had forgotten to make any inquiries on the subject from the doctor. The black looked up at the flag, smiled faintly, and replied with an indifferent air﻿—“Ah! la Republica.”

===Chinese===
威尔逊先生自然也望见了我们，早打发他的船来接我上岸。我把其余的人留在船上，跨进小艇；黑人船夫摇着桨送我从堡下经过时，我忽然想起，便用我所能搜罗出的最像样的葡萄牙语问他，城垛上飘着的那面新旗是什么意思。方才一心惦着检疫的事，竟忘了向港医打听这个。那黑人抬头望了望旗子，淡淡一笑，一副无所谓的神气答道——「啊！共和国（la Republica）。」

===Original===
And so indeed it was﻿—the Republic! When I reached the store, Mr. Wilson told me all about the revolution, which had occurred quite suddenly and quietly on the day after we had last sailed from Bahia. I learned that the much esteemed Emperor had been deposed, and that a Republican form of government had been proclaimed. And a very shabby sort of a revolution it had been, too, for there had been no slaughter, to give an air of dignity and respectability to it. The people themselves appeared to be heartily ashamed of such a feeble thing, and spoke little of it. The most insignificant Republic of Central America could have got up a far more exciting and sanguinary affair at a few hours’ notice. The harbour doctor had not even thought it worth while to mention the change of government when he gave me pratique.

===Chinese===
果不其然——正是共和国！我一到铺子，威尔逊先生便把这场革命的始末原原本本讲给我听：事情就发生在我们上次驶离巴伊亚的第二天，来得又突兀又安静。我得知那位深受敬重的皇帝已被废黜，共和政体业已宣告成立。这场革命也实在寒酸得很，因为连一场杀戮都没有，无从给它添上几分庄严体面的气派。百姓们自己似乎也打心底里为这么一件软塌塌的事害臊，不大谈论它。中美洲最不起眼的一个共和国，临时接到几个钟头的通知，也能闹出一场热闹得多、血腥得多的事端。港医给我检疫放行的时候，甚至觉得这场政府更迭不值一提。

===Original===
No national flag had yet been selected for this latest addition to the list of American Republics, and the flag we saw was that of the State of Bahia. There had been no disturbance in the city when the news of the pronunciamento was telegraphed from Rio. The negroes did not raise a hand to support the Emperor, to whom they owed their freedom. The only incident of note that occurred at Bahia was the salute that was fired at Fort la Mar in honour of the new Government. This salute did cause some little excitement; for, by some mistake, round shot were fired instead of blank cartridges, and one shot went through a longboat swinging on the davits of a Norwegian barque, and did other damage.

===Chinese===
这个新添进美洲共和国名单上的一员，国旗还没有选定，我们望见的那面，是巴伊亚州的州旗。政变文告（pronunciamento）从里约用电报传来时，城里未起半点骚动。黑人没有一个肯抬一抬手去声援皇帝——他们的自由正是拜他所赐。巴伊亚发生的唯一一件值得一提的事，是拉马尔堡为庆贺新政府鸣放的礼炮。这阵礼炮倒真引起了一点小骚动；因为不知哪里弄错了，放出去的不是空包弹，而是实心圆弹，一发炮弹打穿了吊在一艘挪威三桅帆船吊艇架上摇晃的大艇，还造成了些别的损坏。

===Original===
The United States gunboat *Richmond* was at anchor in the bay, awaiting instructions from Washington, it was said, before officially recognising the new sister Republic.

===Chinese===
美国炮艇「里士满号」（Richmond）泊在湾中，据说正候着华盛顿的训示，以便正式承认这位新生的姊妹共和国。

===Original===
The next day was the feast of the Epiphany, a great holiday, and no Brazilian could be got to work under any circumstances whatever. Crackers, rockets, and bells were the order of the day. Even for the two days succeeding the festival these pious people were disinclined to work, and I heard the skippers of vessels raving in Wilson’s store because they could not get the water-boats alongside, or ship their ballast, as the lightermen were still busy letting off crackers in the streets. However, we managed to get all our stores off﻿—oatmeal, plenty of fresh vegetables, fruit, molasses, and a small barrel of *cana* or white rum.

===Chinese===
第二天是主显节，一个大节，无论如何也休想叫一个巴西人干活。爆竹、起花加钟声，便是这一天照例的节目。乃至节后接连两天，这些虔诚的人们仍无心做工；我听见好几艘船的船长在威尔逊的铺子里跳着脚抱怨，因为水船不肯来靠他们的船，压舱物也没法装载——驳船夫们还忙着在街上放爆竹呢。不过，我们总算把要办的给养全都办齐运上了船——燕麦片、大量新鲜蔬菜、水果、糖蜜，还有一小桶「卡纳」（cana）酒，也就是白朗姆。

===Original===
On Thursday, January 9, I renewed my acquaintance with some old friends. The telegraph steamer *Norseman* came into the port. She was still under the command of Captain Lacy, who had taken the *Falcon* in tow with her from Rio to Maldonado nearly ten years before.

===Chinese===
星期四，1 月 9 日，我同几位老朋友重新续上了旧谊。电信公司的汽船「诺斯曼号」（Norseman）进了港，统带她的仍是莱西船长（Captain Lacy）——将近十年前，就是他驾着这条船，把「猎鹰号」从里约拖到了马尔多纳多。

===Original===
We had intended to sail on this day, but the glass had been falling and it was blowing hard from the southeast, so that it seemed advisable to wait for some improvement in the weather. The next day, January 10, the glass began to rise and the sky looked less threatening, the scud no longer rushing across the heavens at a wild pace; so we got underway after breakfast, and once more set sail for the desert island.

===Chinese===
我们本打算当天启航，无奈晴雨表一直在落，东南风又刮得凶，看来还是候一候天气好转为妥。第二天，1 月 10 日，晴雨表开始回升，天色也不那么狰狞了，飞云不再发疯似的掠过天穹；于是我们吃过早饭便动身，重又扬帆驶向那座荒岛。

===Original===
For a vessel sailing from Trinidad to Bahia the wind is always fair, being from northeast to southeast; but for one sailing the reverse way the wind is, as often as not, right ahead. This bad luck we now experienced. Trinidad lay to the southeast of us, and southeast was also the direction of the wind. When we were outside the bay we put the vessel on the port tack and at five in the evening we were off the Moro San Paulo lighthouse. Then we went about and steered away from the land.

===Chinese===
从特林达迪驶往巴伊亚的船，风永远是顺的，不是东北便是东南；可反其道而行的船，风十之八九正顶着头。这股晦气如今轮到我们领受。特林达迪在我们的东南，而风向也正是东南。出了湾，我们让船改走左舷受风，傍晚五点，行至莫罗圣保罗（Moro San Paulo）灯塔外海，随后换舷转向，驶离陆地。

===Original===
This was, I think, our most disagreeable voyage. It blew hard all the time, and there were violent squalls of wind and rain that frequently compelled us to scandalise our mainsail and lower the foresail. The sea ran high, and was very confused, so that, sailing full and by, the yacht made little progress, labouring a good deal, and constantly driving her bowsprit into the short, steep waves. On the third day out we took two reefs down in the mainsail and two in the foresail. The wind was constantly shifting between east and south, so that we often went about so as to sail on the tack which enabled the vessel to point nearest to her destination.

===Chinese===
我想，这是我们最不好受的一趟航行。风从头至尾刮得凶，风雨交加的狂暴阵风频频袭来，屡屡迫得我们束拢主帆、落下前帆。海浪又高又乱，游艇顶着风张满帆抢行，几乎迈不开步，颠簸得苦，艏斜桅一个劲儿地扎进又短又陡的浪头里。出海第三天，我们把主帆收进两道缩帆，前帆也收两道。风一直在东与南之间打转，我们只得频频换舷，好让船始终走在最能把船头指向目的地的那个受风舷上。

===Original===
When we had been six days out we were only halfway to Trinidad, having accomplished the distance of 350 miles from Bahia.

===Chinese===
出海六天，我们才走到去特林达迪的一半路程，从巴伊亚算起，不过前进了 350 英里。

===Original===
On this day I had some trouble with Arthur. He had, I think, brought a bottle of rum on board surreptitiously at Bahia, or, possibly, he had helped himself from the barrel, which was always kept, for security, in my cabin. As I used to sleep on deck during Pollock’s watch, he could then find his opportunity, as no one was below to catch him. At midnight, when I relieved the other watch, he refused to obey an order. He had done this on two previous occasions, also when under the influence of smuggled spirits, and had quickly been brought to his senses and to his work by having his head punched. It was his wont to become repentant and make amends for his bad conduct by extra good behaviour; and I must allow that he did his work willingly enough, as a rule, but drink converted him into a foolish sea-lawyer.

===Chinese===
就在这一天，我同阿瑟起了点风波。我想，他是在巴伊亚偷偷夹带了一瓶朗姆酒上船；要不然，便是从那只桶里自行取用——为稳妥起见，那桶酒一向收在我的舱里。波洛克值更时我惯常睡在甲板上，他便可趁这空当下手，舱里没人抓他。半夜我接班时，他拒不从命。这种事先前已出过两回，也都是在私酒上了头的时候，每回都是脑袋上挨了几拳，便很快清醒过来、回到活计上。他的习性是事后懊悔不迭，格外卖力地表现，将功折罪；我也得承认，平日里他干起活来倒相当情愿，只是一沾酒，就变成了个愚蠢的「海上讼棍」（sea-lawyer）。

===Original===
The offence was flagrant on this occasion, and as a head-punching only resulted in making him sulky, I determined to discharge him. Seeing that months might elapse before we left Trinidad for the West Indies, and not wishing to have him on my hands all that time, I made up my mind to run back to Bahia with him at once; so the mainsheet was promptly slacked off, and we bore away, to the young man’s great surprise. I would not let him go below, in case he should get at the rum again; so ordered him to stay on the deck forward. Before the end of my watch he disobeyed this order and sneaked below in the dark. When I discovered this I went down and ordered him to come on deck at once. He obeyed, promptly this time, as he was, no doubt, reaching the sober and repentant stage; but I would not trust him, and tied him up by his foot to the bulwarks forward, and kept him a prisoner until we came into port.

===Chinese===
这一回他犯得明目张胆，而拳头敲头只会敲得他闷声赌气，我便决意把他辞退。想到我们离开特林达迪驶往西印度群岛，说不定还得几个月，我不想那么久一直把这个包袱背在身上，便拿定主意，即刻带着他折返巴伊亚；于是主帆脚索应声放松，我们转舵顺风驶去，教这位年轻人大为错愕。我不许他下舱，生怕他再够着那朗姆酒；便命令他待在前部甲板上。我这一更还没值完，他又违了令，趁黑溜下舱去。我发觉后下去，命他立时上甲板。这一回他倒是立时照办，想必已到了酒醒悔罪的阶段；可我信不过他了，便把他一只脚拴在前部舷墙上，一路押作囚犯，直到进港。

===Original===
He was the only paid hand we had who was subject to these fits of insubordination. The doctor and myself never had any difficulties with the others; they did their work cheerfully.

===Chinese===
我们雇的水手里，唯独他好犯这种抗命的毛病。医生和我对其余几个，从来没有半点为难；他们干活个个欢欢喜喜。

===Original===
Now that we were running before the wind and sea we made good progress, and we sighted the Moro San Paulo light at 2 a.m. on Sunday, January 19. The distance, therefore, that we had made after six days of tacking was now accomplished before the wind in 50 hours.

===Chinese===
如今船乘着风浪顺跑，进展可观，1 月 19 日星期日凌晨两点，我们望见了莫罗圣保罗灯塔。先前抢风折腾六天方才走完的那段路，如今顺风而下，50 个钟头便到了。

===Original===
We were becalmed off the entrance of the bay for several hours. It was an excessively hot day, and the morning breeze did not spring up till later than usual, so that we did not let go our anchor under Fort la Mar until midday. And now, lo! the flags of the State of Bahia no longer decorated the city and forts, but a flag something like the old Brazilian flag, but yet not the same, floated everywhere. Had there, then, been yet another revolution while we were away, and was some new form of government﻿—communistical or oligarchical or whatnot﻿—being experimented upon? We learnt, on landing, that this was the National flag of the Brazilian Republic, but only a tentative one, which was being flown so that the citizens could see how it looked. I believe several other patterns were tried, and thus exhibited in the cities for public approval, before one was definitely selected.

===Chinese===
湾口外，我们被无风困了几个钟头。那天热得厉害，晨风又起得比往常晚，直到正午，我们才在拉马尔堡下抛了锚。这时，嗬！巴伊亚州的旗子已不再装点城池和炮台，四下飘扬着一面颇像旧巴西国旗、却又不是它的旗子。莫非我们离开的当口又闹了一场革命，什么新式政体——共产的也罢，寡头的也罢，或是什么别的名堂——正拿来试验不成？登岸后我们才得知，这是巴西共和国的国旗，不过是暂定的，挂出来让百姓瞧瞧它是个什么模样。我相信后来又试过别样几种图案，同样在各城张挂出来以征公众之意，最后才正式选定其一。

===Original===
The harbour doctor came off to us, was amused at our story, and again gave us pratique. Wilson had, of course, been much puzzled at the reappearance of the *Alerte*, and was anxious to hear what had happened.

===Chinese===
港医又到船上来，听了我们的故事觉得有趣，再次准了我们检疫放行。威尔逊先生见「阿勒特号」去而复返，自然满腹狐疑，急着要听听究竟。

===Original===
I took Arthur before the Consul on Monday morning, and formally discharged him.

===Chinese===
星期一早晨，我把阿瑟带到领事面前，正式将他解雇。

===Original===
New brooms sweep clean, they say, and the new Republican Municipality had decided to clean dirty Bahia as economically as possible, and had hit upon the following ingenious plan. The police were instructed to consider anyone, whatever his rank, who was found walking in the streets after bedtime, as a dangerous conspirator, and to promptly arrest him. All men locked up on any night for this crime were sent out the next morning in a gang to sweep the streets. It was interesting, I was told, to observe some gay young Brazilian masher, in silk hat, lofty collar, and pointed patent boots, cleaning a gutter out, with an armed policeman standing over him to see that he did not shirk his work. I was instructed by the Consul to warn any of my men who should come on shore as to the danger of strolling about the city at night.

===Chinese===
常言道，新官上任三把火；新上台的共和派市政当局决意用最省钱的法子打扫肮脏的巴伊亚，还想出了下面这条妙计。警察奉命把任何在就寝时辰之后仍上街行走的人——无论他地位高低——一概视作危险的阴谋家，随即逮捕。无论哪一夜因这条罪名被收押的人，第二天一早都被编成一队，赶出去扫街。据说那光景颇有看头：某个油头粉面的年轻巴西纨绔子，丝礼帽、高立领、尖头漆皮靴，正躬身清理着阴沟，旁边立着一名持枪警察，盯住他不许偷懒。领事嘱我告诫手下：凡要上岸的，都须提防夜里在城中游荡的风险。

===Original===
I did not wish to remain at Bahia one moment longer than was necessary; but I thought it would be well, as we were here, to fill up our water-tanks. But it happened to be another fiesta this day﻿—bells and crackers again!﻿—and the water-boat could not come off. So we had to wait till the following day, January 21, when the water was put on board of us, and in the afternoon we got underway.

===Chinese===
我在巴伊亚一刻也不愿多留，办完事就走；不过既然来了，我想不如把水舱灌满。不巧这天又赶上一个节庆（fiesta）——又是钟声加爆竹！——水船来不了。我们只好候到第二天，1 月 21 日，淡水装上了船，下午我们便启程了。

## XIX / 十九

===Original===
Hove To

===Chinese===
顶风停船

===Original===
It was blowing hard on the day of our departure from Bahia, and we sailed down the bay under mizzen and head sails, so as to see what it was like outside before hoisting our mainsail.

===Chinese===
我们驶离巴伊亚的那天，风刮得正紧，我们只张着后帆和艏帆顺湾而下，好先看看外海是什么风浪，再决定升不升主帆。

===Original===
A high sea was running on the bar, and while the yacht was tumbling about in the broken water, an accident happened to Wright. He was preparing our tea, when a lurch of the vessel capsized a kettle of boiling water, the whole contents of which poured over his hands and wrists, scalding them severely, and causing intense pain; so that we had to administer a strong sleeping draught to the poor fellow, after the usual remedies had been applied to the scalded parts. He was on the sick list for a long time, and was, of course, incapable of doing work of any description during this voyage; though, as soon as he got a bit better, it worried him to think that he was of no use, and he insisted, though his hands were bandaged up, in trying to steer with his arms.

===Chinese===
拦门沙上巨浪滚滚，游艇在碎浪里颠簸打滚之际，赖特出了事故。他正在给我们预备茶，船身猛地一歪，一壶滚沸的开水翻倒下来，整壶水尽数泼在他的手上腕上，烫得极重，疼痛钻心；烫伤处敷过惯常的方子之后，我们还只得给这可怜人服下一剂强力的安眠药水。此后很长时间他都列名病号册上，这一航程里自然什么活计都干不得了；不过他稍见好转，一想到自己成了无用之人便满心焦躁，纵然两手裹满绷带，也执意要用臂膀试着掌舵。

===Original===
This accident made us still more short-handed. There were but three of us left to work the vessel. Luckily, I had one good man with me, in the person of Ted Milner, who not only did the cooking, but worked hard on deck during my watch as well as on the other, and was very cheery over it all the while, too.

===Chinese===
这场事故使我们的人手愈发短绌。船上只剩三个人操船。所幸我身边还有一条好汉，便是特德·米尔纳：他不但包揽了做饭，无论我这一更还是另一更，都在甲板上卖力干活，而且自始至终乐呵呵的。

===Original===
When we were outside, we took two reefs down in the mainsail before hoisting it, and close-reefed the foresail, for it was evident that we were in for a spell of squally weather.

===Chinese===
驶到湾外，我们先给主帆打下两道缩帆再升起来，前帆则收作满缩帆，因为明摆着我们要赶上一阵多狂风的天气了。

===Original===
We had better luck now than during our previous attempt at reaching Trinidad, for the wind, instead of being right ahead from the southeast, kept shifting backwards and forwards between north and east, so that we could always lay our course on the port tack, and could often do so with our sheets well off. But the wind was squally and uncertain, and for much of the time the sea was rough, so that we were eight days in reaching the island.

===Chinese===
这一回我们的运气比上次驶往特林达迪时强多了：风不再从东南正顶着船头，而是在北与东之间来回摆动，我们总能以左舷受风贴着航向走，往往还能把帆脚索放得很松。只是风一阵一阵、捉摸不定，多半时候海面又粗恶，我们走了八天才到岛。

===Original===
At dawn on January 29, we sighted Trinidad, right ahead, and in the afternoon we were about two miles off, opposite to the Ninepin rock. It was blowing hard from the eastward, and the sea was, I think, running higher than on any occasion since we left Southampton. The surf on the island was far heavier than we had ever seen it before, and was breaking on every portion of the coast with great fury.

===Chinese===
1 月 29 日拂晓，我们望见特林达迪正在正前方；到了下午，我们离岛约两英里，正对着九柱石。风从东面猛刮过来，我想，自离开南安普敦以来，还不曾见海面哪一回涌得这样高。岛上的碎浪比我们历来所见的都凶猛得多，正以雷霆万钧之势在海岸的每一个地段上崩裂。

===Original===
We now ran before the wind towards Southwest Bay, and the squalls that occasionally swept down the ravines were so fierce that we sailed with foresail down and the tack of our reefed mainsail triced well up. We saw that the seas were dashing completely over the pier, and sending great fountains of spray high into the air. When we opened out Southwest Bay the scene before us was terribly grand. Huge green rollers, with plumes of snowy spray, were breaking on the sandy beach; and the waves were dashing up the sides of Noah’s Ark, and the Sugarloaf to an immense height, the cliffs being wet with spray quite 200 feet up. The loud roaring of the seas was echoed by the mountains, and the frequent squalls whistled and howled frightfully among the crags, so that even the wild seabirds were alarmed at the commotion of the elements: for they had risen in multitudes from all the rocks around the bay, and were flying hither and thither in a scared fashion, while their melancholy cries added to the weirdness of the general effect.

===Chinese===
我们于是顺风驶向西南湾，不时顺着条条裂谷扫落下来的狂风极为凶猛，我们只得落下前帆航行，把打了缩帆的主帆受风下角高高吊起。我们望见巨浪整个扑过天然码头，把一股股巨大的水花喷泉高高送上空中。西南湾豁然展开时，眼前的景象雄浑得骇人。绿色巨浪顶着雪白浪花的羽冠，在沙滩上崩碎；波浪扑上「诺亚方舟」和糖面包山的崖壁，激溅到惊人的高度，崖石被浪花浸湿，直漫上足有 200 英尺高。海涛的轰响在群山间回荡，阵阵狂风在嶙峋峭壁间凄厉地呼啸怒号，连野性惯了的海鸟也被这天地的一场大骚动惊扰了：它们从湾四周所有的礁岩上大批大批飞起，惶惶然东飞西窜，一声声凄楚的啼鸣，更为这满目森然的景象平添几分诡谲。

===Original===
And once more we saw before us, high above the sea-foam, our little camp, with its three tents, and the whaleboat hauled up on the sands not far off, with its white canvas cover stretched over it; but we were surprised to see no men about: the camp appeared to be deserted.

===Chinese===
又一次，我们望见我们那小小的营地现于眼前，高踞于浪花飞沫之上：三顶帐篷立在那里，不远处沙滩上拖放着捕鲸艇，白色的帆布罩绷在艇上；可四下不见人影，令我们好生诧异：这营地看去竟像已被弃置。

===Original===
It was, obviously, impossible for the shore-party to launch the boat with so high a sea running, neither could we approach within signalling distance of the beach; so that there was no chance of our being able to communicate with our friends for the present. I also saw that it would be highly imprudent, if not impossible, to come to an anchor off the cascade with the yacht. There was to be no harbour for us just yet, and the only thing to be done was to put to sea and heave to until the weather improved.

===Chinese===
明摆着，涌浪这样高，岸上队伍没法把小艇放下水；我们也驶不进能同海滩互打信号的距离之内；因此眼下一时无从与我们的朋友们通消息。我也看出，驾着游艇到瀑布外下锚，即便不是全然办不到，也是极不妥当的。眼下我们是无港可投，唯一的办法只有驶向外海，顶风停船，静候天气好转。

===Original===
We did not anticipate that we should have to wait long for this improvement; but, as it turned out, we had to remain hove to for eight days, before the state of the sea permitted the boat to come off to us, during which time the bananas, pumpkins, and other luxuries of the sort, which we had brought from Bahia for the working-party, began to spoil, and we had to eat them ourselves to save them; so that, when at last the men boarded us, we had but little left for them of the fresh fruit and vegetables which were so grateful to them, though of oatmeal and other provisions there was an ample store.

===Chinese===
我们原不曾料到要等这么久才有好转；谁知后来竟顶风停船停了八天，海况才容许小艇驶到我们船上来。这期间，我们从巴伊亚给工作队捎来的香蕉、南瓜等一类难得的吃食开始腐坏，我们只好自己吃掉以免糟蹋；待到人们终于上船时，那些极受他们欢迎的新鲜果菜，我们已经剩不下多少给他们了，好在燕麦片和其他给养存量充裕。

===Original===
We soon discovered that it was much better in every way for the yacht to be hove to than to be lying at anchor off Trinidad. To strain at her chain in an ocean swell must be injurious even to such a strong vessel as the *Alerte* is; and, as I have said, we did pull one hawsepipe nearly out of her on the occasion that the chain got foul of the rocks at the bottom, thus giving her a short nip. Even in fine weather we experienced a lot of wear and tear; for the yacht used to swing first in one direction, then in another, as the various flaws of wind struck her, so that the chain was constantly getting round her stem, and we found that a large piece of her copper had been worn away in this manner, just below the waterline.

===Chinese===
我们很快便发觉，游艇顶风停船，比起泊在特林达迪外抛锚，方方面面都强得多。在大洋涌浪里绷紧锚链，即便对「阿勒特号」这样坚固的船，也必有损伤；何况如我先前所说，那一回锚链缠上海底的礁石、船身被紧紧咬住了一下，我们几乎把一只锚链筒都从船头拽脱了出来。便是天气晴好，磨损也够我们受的：一阵阵不规则的风扑到船上，游艇忽而荡向这边、忽而荡向那边，锚链便不住地缠到船首柱上去，我们发现水线之下不远处，一大片包船的铜皮就是这样磨蚀掉的。

===Original===
Had I fully realised before the great advantages of heaving to, I do not think I should have ever let go my anchor at all here; but, in that case, I should have been compelled to remain on board all the while, and would not have had my fortnight’s stay in camp. To remain hove to off this lee side of the island is a very easy matter. Our method was to sail out to sea from Southwest Bay until we had got out of the baffling local squalls into the steady breeze, and then we hove to under reefed mainsail, small jib with sheet to windward, and helm lashed. The yacht then looked after herself; and, as the wind was always more or less off shore and the current was setting to the south, she would drift away about twelve miles in the night towards the open sea, always remaining right opposite our bay, so that those on shore could see us at daybreak. We divided ourselves into three watches at night, one man being sufficient for a watch, for he never had anything to do but lookout for the passing vessels. Hove to as we were under such short canvas the fiercest squall we ever encountered had no effect on the vessel, and she was in every way very comfortable.

===Chinese===
早先若充分明白顶风停船的这许多好处，我想我在这里根本就不会抛过一回锚；不过真那样的话，我就只得自始至终守在船上，不会有营地里那两个星期了。在这岛背风一面之外顶风停船，是桩轻而易举的事。我们的办法是：从西南湾驶向外海，直到钻出此地四处乱窜的阵风、进入稳定的风里，然后顶着打了缩帆的主帆、一面帆脚索勒向上风的小艏帆停船，舵柄缚牢。此后游艇自会照管自己；由于风或多或少总从岸上吹来，海流又向南流，她夜里会朝外海漂出约十二英里，却始终正对着我们那处海湾，岸上的人破晓时便望得见我们。夜里我们分三班当值，一班一人尽够，因为当值的无事可做，无非瞭望过往的船只而已。我们收着这样短的帆顶风停着，纵使撞上所遇过的最凶的狂风，船也毫发无恙，方方面面都安顿得舒舒服服。

===Original===
In the morning we would hoist the foresail and tack towards Southwest Bay, so as to attempt communication with the shore; if that were impossible, we hove to once more, to drift slowly seawards; and we repeated this process several times in the course of a day, before we finally sailed out for our night’s rest on the bosom of the ocean.

===Chinese===
清晨我们便扬起前帆，抢风驶向西南湾，试着与岸上通消息；若办不到，便再度顶风停船，缓缓向海上漂去；一天之内这般往返数回，最后才驶向外海，到大洋的怀抱里安歇一夜。

===Original===
We could sail into Southwest Bay until we were abreast of the Sugarloaf, but no further; we were then at least a mile and a quarter from the camp, and it was difficult to read the signals of the shore-party at that distance, as the flags they had with them were of a small size.

===Chinese===
西南湾我们能一直驶到与糖面包山并齐之处，再往前便进不得；那时我们离营地至少还有一又四分之一英里，隔着这样的距离，岸上队伍打的信号便难以辨认，因为他们随身的旗子都小。

===Original===
To have approached nearer than this would have been a very risky proceeding; for, though we might have succeeded in getting some way further in, and out again, with safety, time after time, the day would most assuredly have come when a serious accident would have happened. For, as soon as the yacht had sailed across the line connecting the two extreme points of the bay, the high cliffs diverted the wind so that it was only felt occasionally, and then in short squalls, from various directions; and between those baffling squalls were long spells of calm, during which the vessel would drift helplessly before the swell towards the surf under the cliffs, or would be carried by the southerly current towards the lava reefs off South Point, in both cases at imminent risk of destruction. And even when the squalls did come down to render assistance, they shifted so suddenly that the sails were taken aback two or three times in as many minutes, so that all way was lost, or even stern way was got on the vessel, and one lost control over her at a critical moment.

===Chinese===
比这再往里逼近，便是极冒失的举动；因为虽说我们或许能一回又一回安然再往里驶上一程、再退出来，但那一天几乎笃定会来——来了便是大祸临头。游艇一驶过连接湾之两极点的连线，高崖便把风搅散了，风只是偶尔才吹到，而且都是短促的阵风，方向五花八门；阵风与阵风之间夹着长长的无风时段，其间船在涌浪面前无计可施，只顾朝着崖脚的碎浪漂去，或者被南向的海流带向南角外的熔岩礁，两样都是倾覆之祸迫在眉睫。便算阵风果然下来救急，它们转向又转得极突然，几分钟里帆便被顶反两三回，船的冲力尽失，甚至生出退势，教人在紧要关头失掉对她的掌控。

===Original===
The *Alerte* sailed into that bay a great many times without mishap; but there were anxious moments now and then, and I was always glad to escape out of this treacherous trap to the open sea, clear of the rocks and squalls, with deep water round, and a comparatively steady wind to help me.

===Chinese===
「阿勒特号」驶进那湾不知多少回，从未失事；但间或也有令人心悬的时刻，而每逃出这个阴险的陷阱、回到外海——四周深水环绕，脱开了礁石与阵风，还有一阵比较稳定的风替我帮衬——我总归是欢喜的。

===Original===
We remained thus, standing off and on, and hove to, during the rest of our stay at Trinidad. Our anchor was never let go here again. We had been lucky with our weather when we first arrived at the island, and had successively landed our working-party and stores, and our whaleboat had been beached in Southwest Bay a good many times, without serious accident, though very seldom without risk. But now all this was changed. High seas and squally weather were the rule during the eighteen days we remained hove to: for the first eight days, as I have said, we were unable to hold communication with the shore; and, after that, there were but few occasions on which we could beach the boat, and then this feat was generally attended with a capsize, loss of property, and risk of life. But, fortunately, as will be seen, the two days preceding our final departure from the islet were fine, and we were thus enabled to carry off our tents and other stores. Had it not been for this short spell of calm, we should have probably been compelled to leave behind everything we possessed.

===Chinese===
我们在特林达迪余下的日子里就这样往返徘徊、顶风停船，在这里再没有抛过一回锚。初到岛时我们天公作美，接连把工作队和给养送上了岸，捕鲸艇也在西南湾抢了不知多少回滩，未出大事故——虽然极少有不担风险的时候。可如今这一切全变了。我们顶风停船的十八天里，恶浪与狂风成了家常便饭：头八天，如前所述，我们无法与岸上通消息；此后，能让小艇抢滩的时机也寥寥无几，而且每回这一壮举多半要搭上一场倾覆、若干财物的损失，还有性命之危。所幸——下文自见分晓——我们最终离岛之前的两天天气晴好，我们才得以把帐篷和其他给养运走。若没有这段短短的平静日子，我们恐怕就不得不把身边所有的一切都丢在身后了。

===Original===
The fine season here is in the southern summer﻿—our winter. In winter﻿—especially in the months of June, July, and August﻿—landing on Trinidad is almost always impossible. Strong winds and heavy rains then prevail, while the seas run high. It is possible that the fine weather was now beginning to break up, and that when we sailed from the island﻿—February 15﻿—the stormy autumn season was setting in.

===Chinese===
这里的晴季在南半球的夏季——也就是我们的冬季。冬季里——尤其六月、七月、八月这三个月——登上特林达迪几乎全无可能。那时狂风暴雨交相肆虐，海浪高涨。也许眼下的晴好天气已开始瓦解，我们驶离该岛之时——2 月 15 日——多风暴的秋季已在登场了。

===Original===
The ship’s log for this period presents a monotonous repetition of vain attempts at boating, as the following short record of our proceedings for the first eight days will show. It will be remembered that we arrived off the island and hove to on the evening of January 29.

===Chinese===
这一时期的航海日志，尽是一段又一段徒劳的放艇尝试，单调地重复，下面这头八天行事的小小记录可见一斑。诸位当还记得，我们是 1 月 29 日傍晚抵达岛外、顶风停船的。

===Original===
January 30.﻿—Sailed into Southwest Bay after breakfast. Though we saw the camp standing as we had left it, could not perceive any men, neither had we done so on the previous day. Wonder if, for some reason or other, the shore-party have left the island, and been carried away by a passing vessel? Drift out of bay and heave to. In afternoon sail into bay again. This time are glad to see all the men walking down to the beach. We signal for news. They reply, “All well,” and “Too rough for boating.” We signal, that we have brought them some letters from Bahia. When outside bay heave to for night.

===Chinese===
1 月 30 日——早饭后驶入西南湾。营地仍是我们离去时的模样，却一个人影也望不见，前一日也是如此。莫非岸上队伍不知出于什么缘故，已经离了岛，被过路的船只带走了？驶出湾外，顶风停船。下午再驶入湾中。这一回喜见全体人员正走向海滩。我们打出旗语探问消息。他们回答：「一切安好」「浪太猛，放不了艇」。我们打信号说，从巴伊亚给他们捎了信件来。驶出湾外，顶风停船过夜。

===Original===
January 31.﻿—At dawn ten miles off island. Tack towards island. Sea high; squally. Sail into bay. No signals from shore. We conclude it is too rough for boating, and that the men are at work in the ravine. In afternoon sail again into bay. No signals. Heave to for night, as before.

===Chinese===
1 月 31 日——拂晓时离岛十英里。抢风驶向岛去。浪高；风一阵一阵。驶入湾中。岸上没有信号。我们断定浪太猛放不得艇，人们都在裂谷里干活。下午又驶入湾中。没有信号。照旧顶风停船过夜。

===Original===
February 1.﻿—Sail into bay in morning. See the men on shore taking the cover off the whaleboat, as if with the intention of coming off. They drag her down to the edge of the sea. We cannot now distinguish them, so cannot tell whether they have launched the boat or not, or whether they have capsized, or what may have happened. All is hidden from us for some time; then we see them hauling the boat up the beach again. They have evidently abandoned the attempt as too dangerous. Very squally. While hove to, drive a long way from island. In evening, sail towards the bay again and heave to for night.

===Chinese===
2 月 1 日——早晨驶入湾中。望见岸上的人们正揭去捕鲸艇的罩布，像是要放艇到我们这边来。他们把艇拖下到浪边。此刻我们已辨认不清他们，无从知道艇到底放下水了没有，是不是翻了，或是出了什么别的事。一切对我们隐没了一阵；随后我们望见他们又在把艇往沙滩上拖。他们显然嫌这尝试太险，放弃了。狂风阵阵。顶风停船期间，被风吹离岛很远。傍晚，重又驶向湾去，顶风停船过夜。

===Original===
February 2.﻿—Heavy showers of rain obscuring island from our view. Enter bay in morning. It being Sunday no work is done in the ravine, but the shore-party make many fruitless attempts at launching the boat during the day. We stand in and out of the bay all day, watching the proceedings of those on shore through our glasses. On several occasions the men draw the boat down to the edge of the sea, disappear from our sight for a time, and at last reappear hauling the boat up again. They persevere despite repeated failures. Think they have capsized once at least, as they are baling the boat out on the beach. At last, at 4 p.m., they give up the attempt as hopeless, and hoist the signal: “Impossible to launch lifeboat.” We exchange several signals, but find it difficult to distinguish their small flags from the yacht. At sunset we sail out to sea and heave to. Choppy sea. Tumble about a good deal. Stormy-looking sky.

===Chinese===
2 月 2 日——暴雨阵阵，岛影从我们眼前隐没。早晨进湾。这天是星期日，裂谷里不做工，岸上队伍却一整日里一次次徒劳地试着放艇。我们整日在湾中驶进驶出，用望远镜观看岸上人们的举动。有好几回，人们把艇拖到浪边，从我们视野里消失片刻，末了又现身把艇往上拖。他们屡败屡战。想来至少翻过一回船，因为他们正在滩上往外舀艇里的水。最后，下午 4 点，他们认了输，放弃这无望的尝试，升起信号：「无法放出救生艇」。我们往返互打了几个信号，只是从游艇这边很难辨清他们那面小旗。日落时分我们驶向外海，顶风停船。海面碎乱。颠簸得不轻。天空一副风暴将至的模样。

===Original===
February 3.﻿—This morning very clear; so see distinctly for first time the three rocky islets of Martin Vas, distant about twenty-five miles from Trinidad, bearing east. Sail into bay. Again several vain attempts to launch boat. Heave to. Drift this night upwards of fifteen miles from island.

===Chinese===
2 月 3 日——今晨天色极清；于是头一回清清楚楚望见马丁瓦斯群岛的三座岩岛，在特林达迪以东约二十五英里处。驶入湾中。又是几次徒劳的放艇尝试。顶风停船。这一夜漂离岛屿十五英里有余。

===Original===
February 4.﻿—Sail into bay. Still high surf. A signal flying on shore which we cannot distinguish, so sail somewhat nearer in. Are becalmed under Sugarloaf. Then a squall﻿—then taken aback by another squall﻿—then calm again. We drift towards Noah’s Ark, up whose face the sea is breaking fifty or sixty feet high. Another squall; wear vessel and clear out of bay. A very squally day, with baffling winds making it more than usually dangerous to enter the bay.

===Chinese===
2 月 4 日——驶入湾中。碎浪依然高猛。岸上飘着一面我们辨不出的信号旗，于是稍稍驶近了些。到了糖面包山脚下便陷入无风。接着一阵狂风——又被另一阵狂风顶反了帆——复归于无风。我们朝「诺亚方舟」漂去，海浪正扑上它那面崖壁，掀起五六十英尺高。又来一阵狂风；掉转船头，驶出湾去。这日狂风阵阵，乱风离谱，进湾比平常凶险得多。

---

===Original===
At last, on February 5, after having made three vain attempts to cross the barrier of tumbling surf, the whaleboat was successfully launched, and we saw her come out safely from the line of breakers at the end of the bay; then the men pulled away towards us, visible one moment as the boat rose to the top of the swell, and hidden the next moment from our sight by the rollers as she sank into the valleys between them.

===Chinese===
2 月 5 日，在三次穿越翻滚碎浪屏障的徒劳尝试之后，捕鲸艇终于成功下了水，我们望见她安然驶出湾尾那一列浪头；随后人们朝我们划来，小艇随涌浪升上浪顶的一刻显一显身，跌进浪谷的下一刻又被巨浪从我们眼前藏了起来。

===Original===
We sailed into the bay to meet her, and hove to abreast of the Sugarloaf. The boat came nearer, and we saw that the doctor, Powell, Pursell, and the two black men, were in her. It was now thirty-eight days since we had last seen our companions. They all looked gaunt and haggard, and were clad in flannel shirts and trousers, ragged and earth-stained from the work in the ravine.

===Chinese===
我们驶进湾中迎她，在糖面包山旁顶风停船。小艇近来，我们看清艇里是医生、鲍威尔、珀塞尔和那两名黑人。距我们上一回见到这些同伴，已经三十八天了。他们个个形容枯瘦憔悴，身上穿的法兰绒衬衫和裤子，被裂谷里的活计磨得破烂，沾满泥土。

===Original===
But they were the same cheery boys as ever, as I discovered by the jovial manner of their greeting as soon as they were within hail. “Hullo!” sang out the doctor, “what vessel’s that, and where do you come from? I am the doctor of the port here. Hand over your bill of health, that I may see whether you can have pratique.”

===Chinese===
但他们还是从前那班乐天派，他们一进呐喊可闻的距离、那副兴致勃勃寒暄的样子便让我看了出来。「喂！」医生扬声喊道，「这是哪条船，打哪儿来？我是本港的港医。把你们的健康证书交出来，让我瞧瞧能不能准你们检疫放行。」

===Original===
“And I am the governor of this island of Trinidad,” cried Powell, with affable pompousness from under an extraordinary hat that had been manufactured by himself, apparently out of the remains of old hampers and bird’s-nests; “will you do me the honour of dining with me at Government House tonight? I shall be glad to learn from you how the revolution is progressing in our neighbouring State of Brazil. I was just on the point of sending out my squadron here”﻿—patting the whaleboat on the side﻿—“to Bahia, to look after the interests of any of our subjects who may be there.”

===Chinese===
「而我呢，是这座特林达迪岛的总督，」鲍威尔在一顶了不得的帽子底下和蔼可亲而又煞有介事地嚷道——那帽子显然是他亲手制作的，用料看来是旧提篮和鸟窝的残骸——「可否赏光，今晚到总督府与我共进晚餐？我很乐意从您这儿听听，我们邻邦巴西的革命进展如何。我正要把我的舰队派出去」——说着拍拍捕鲸艇的艇侧——「开往巴伊亚，去照看我们可能侨居在那里的臣民们的利益。」

===Original===
It was startling for us to find that these dwellers on a desert island had already heard of the Brazilian revolution, and we were still more amazed when they proved to us that they were well informed as to all that had been going on in the outer world. We had been looking forward to imparting the latest news to them, but lo! all that we had to tell was stale to them. They kept us in a state of mystification for some time before they revealed the source of this marvellous knowledge, and the only information that Powell would vouchsafe us on the subject was to the effect that:﻿—“We found it slow here without the newspapers at breakfast, and have established telegraphic communication with England. All the latest racing intelligence comes through the tape in the doctor’s tent.” But, before asking any questions, we greeted our long-absent friends. They came on board and had a good square meal, such as they had not enjoyed for a long time, with red wine, cigars, and other luxuries, and after this we sat down to a long yarn and an exchange of news.

===Chinese===
